The connector also closes shapes that the rule lets through, each failing closed: a group after a comma that begins with `and`, `&` or `+` goes on with the group before it; the `Last, First` shape drops the whole party, and with a surname that is no bare word (two to four words, a suffix such as `Jr.` or `III`, a particle or `Estate of`) the person's group, the given name that follows it and everything after the given name go, while what stands before the person stays; the particles `de`, `del`, `la`, `las`, `los`, `el`, `en` and the `y` are taken out of a group before the rule judges it; `doing business as`, `trading as` and `t/a` split a name as `d/b/a` does, so that the person before them is dropped and the business after them stays; a group of legal wording alone (`individually`, `jointly and severally`) is no party; a name that goes on under another name (`a/k/a`, `aka`, `f/k/a`, `n/k/a`) is the same defendant's, and when every principal party is dropped only the business that a `d/b/a`, `dba`, `t/a`, `doing business as` or `trading as` names is promoted; for the person shape only (the stored text is never changed), every quotation mark of Unicode is read as a quotation mark and every dash as a hyphen; the head of a name ends at a dash that separates (a blank, one or two dashes of any script, a blank); a nickname in parentheses, brackets or braces (one or two capitalized words or initials) is left out of what the rule judges, and a name with a parenthesis is judged as it was always read and as it is named, a group staying only if both readings keep it; and a piece that holds `Estate of`, or a name of more than one word that ends in `Estate`, is a person's. The costs of these rules, which are accepted: a firm named as two one-word brands in a row, a one-word brand that stands before a person, a firm of one word, a parenthesis and a word with no legal form, a firm that holds `Estate of`, and a US place name that begins with a Spanish particle and has no legal form are dropped. Left open, and stored as the rules give it: a person's name of five words or more once its particles are out; a person's name with a digit; a join with no blank round it; a `y` between a company with a legal form and a person, since the legal form wins; a person whose surname is a word of the kit's lists (an organization word or a legal form); the words `with`, `et`, `und` and `e`, which join nothing; a trust that names its trustee or is named after a person, which is stored; a parenthesis that is no nickname, which is cut as ever so that its head is stored; a nickname inside the name of a company, which stores the first word as the entity; a bracket or a brace that holds a name after a company or an alias mark, which stays in the name; a run of three dashes, or a dash that touches the word after it; a colon, a vertical bar, a tilde, a bullet, a middle dot or the CJK corner brackets between a company and a person; and estate wordings other than the two above. On the real file of 2026-11-10 the closures lose one record: a firm named as a list behind a group of a person's shape, lost to the given-name rule.
  - **Natural persons: the Colombian sanctions.** About a quarter of the rows fine a person, and one cell may name several parties. The Spanish rule takes no cell that holds a `/` or an `&`, or a company beside a person, for a person's name as a whole, so the connector judges the cleaned cell twice before anything is stored. As a whole, a cell with the shape of a person's name drops its row. Side by side, the cell is split on ` Y/O `, on a hyphen or a dash with a blank on one side at least, on a slash, and on ` Y `, ` E `, ` & ` and ` + ` with a blank on each side, and each side is judged on its own without its tails (`EN LIQUIDACION`, `CON SIGLA`), since a company has them and an organization word in them would hide the shape of a person's name. A side with the shape of a person's name is removed, tails and all; when none is left the row is dropped, and when some are the party of the row is the sides that remain, joined by ` Y/O `, and the cell that names the person is never a name, an id, a display, a title, a part of the text, an identifier or a key. When sides were removed, the row is made only if a remaining side can be matched by a vendor (four letters and digits once its legal form is off, and a word that is no legal form, no word of an organization, no particle and no word of a family firm): a person beside `HERMANOS`, `LTDA`, `CIA S EN C` or `HIJOS` alone leaves no company, and the row is dropped, counted nowhere. The rule is asked of a text whose typographic apostrophes are made ASCII and whose trailing parenthesis or number of identity (`C.C.`, `CC`, `NIT` and its number) is taken off, since what follows a person's name holds words that no name has. What the rule does not see stays in the party: a name with a letter that Spanish does not use, a name of more than six words (the particles of Spanish count as words), the compound surname of a person that holds a ` Y `, and a person behind a hyphen or a dash with no blank on either side, a parenthesis or a comma, which join nothing, a side of one word, and a person's name that holds an organization word. A company of two plain words that ends in a parenthesis reads as a person's with the parenthesis off, and its row is lost; no cell of the real list of 2026-11-10 is one.
  - **Natural persons: the California breach notices.** The two rules (`californiaPracticeRule`: a leading Dr., a leading Law Office or Law Offices of, a credential after the last comma or as the last word of a name of three words or more; `CALIFORNIA_MIDDLE_INITIAL_NAME`: first name, middle initial, last name) read a name as a whole, and a person can be named in a part of one, so each part is judged on its own before the name is used: the parts that an alias mark sets apart, the text inside each pair of parentheses and the text outside them. A part with the shape of a person is cut out of the name with the mark or the parentheses that held it, the spelling of the row is what remains, and the name as it was written is never an id, a display, a title, a part of the text, an identifier or a key; when nothing remains, the row is dropped, like the row of a person, and counted nowhere. Brackets that do not pair up one to one cannot be taken apart with certainty, so a person between any two of them takes the whole part with it, and the text of a pair is judged as it is and again with the alias marks that open it taken off, so that a mark never hides a person. A person's practice written as the pieces of one name, behind a comma and a blank or an ampersand with a blank on each side, is dropped when a piece that remains has the shape of a person and no word of the name is a legal form or a word of an organization; a family firm that carries one is kept, which is the cost of the rule. What the rules do not see stays in the name: the middle-initial rule reads ASCII letters only and the whole name from its first letter to its last, so a name with an accented letter, in lower case, behind a title such as `Mr.`, with a particle, last name first behind a comma, or with a possessive is no person's by it; an alias mark written another way (`d.b.a.`, `dba:`, `doing business as`, `t/a`) splits nothing (six names of the list of 2026-11-10 hold one); a comma or an ampersand with no blank, `and` and a hyphen join nothing; and a few plain two-word names of persons are not caught, so that the record of one is offered only to a vendor that has exactly that name.
  - **Natural persons: the Dutch register.** The one rule is the academic title or honorific, which marks a natural person; the other rule that was tried, two or three capitalized words, found no person in 22 hits and is not applied, and the 4 provider cells (3 distinct texts) of the real file that carried a title (counted on 2026-11-10) were all persons. A title is a word at the start of a name or after a blank, a colon, a semicolon, a comma or a bracket (a square one too), in any case and whatever follows it: `prof`, `dr`, `drs`, `ir`, `mr`, `dhr`, `mevr` and `mevrouw`, and `ing` and `bc` with a full stop (a bare `ING` is the bank). A post-nominal after a name (`MSc`, `MBA`, `PhD`, `LLM` and `RA`, with or without the full stops) is a marker too, as a whole word in the piece or as the text of brackets; a name that begins with the letters of one (`MSC Zorbex`) is a company's. A title that follows the name it qualifies is the same person: brackets whose text begins with a title drop the piece they belong to, and a piece that begins with a title, or has such brackets, drops the piece before it when a comma separated the two (the surname of a name written last name first); a title that stands after initials, or after more pieces, takes with it the comma-separated pieces before it that are initials or one capitalized word, back to a piece that holds a legal form or an organization word, has two capitalized words or a lower-case word, or that another separator set apart. A semicolon, a slash and the word `en` separate two parties, so that a company beside a person stays. The rule fails closed: a company whose name starts with a title word, is followed by a post-nominal or ends in `LLM`, `MBA` or `RA` is dropped as a person is, and a company in front of a title behind a comma is lost with it, since a comma is also what separates a surname from its title. Left open: a title glued to initials, a name with a particle or of two capitalized words before a title, a surname or an initial in lower case, a title in later brackets or after other words of a piece that does not begin with one, a hyphenated initial, `mw.`, `t.a.v.` before a person, the post-nominals `BSc`, `MA`, `MD`, `RC`, `RE` and `EMIA`, and every person with no title at all.
  - **Natural persons: the C2PA list.** The kit has no rule for this list, and the connector applies its own. A product whose applicant holds one of the terms that make it the business of a person is dropped as soon as its applicant is read, whatever else is wrong with it, and counts nowhere, not even as unreadable. The terms are tested in any case: `sole propriet` and `sole trader`, `einzelunternehm`, `einzelkaufmann` and `einzelkauffrau`, `freelanc` and `self employed` (in the terms of two words the two words may stand apart by up to three blanks or dashes of any kind), the German `e.K`, `e. K`, `e.Kfm` and `e.Kfr` and the Austrian `e.U.` as words of their own, `Einzelfirma`, `eenmanszaak`, `entreprise individuelle`, `EIRL`, `auto-entrepreneur`, `micro-entrepreneur`, `empresario individual`, `autonomo`, `ditta individuale`, `enskild firma` and `enkeltpersonforetak` as whole words, and `Sole Prop.` and `Inh.`; `EI`, of two letters, is no term. The same terms keep an organization name out of the keys, and a company whose name holds one is dropped too. A person who trades under a business name is that business: in an applicant `<name>, dba <business>` (also `d/b/a`, `doing business as`, `t/a` and `trading as`) the part before the first mark is cut out of everything the record holds when it holds a letter or a digit and no legal form and no word of an organization (the words that join the parts of a name, such as `and` and `of`, are no organization words), and a mark with nothing readable after it leaves a person's business, which is dropped. Left open: a sole trader who does not say so, or says it in a form that is not listed (four separators between the words, a minus sign for a dash); the other forms of the marks (`dba:`, `d.b.a.`, a mark inside brackets, `operating as`, `o/a`, `trading under`) and the reverse order `<business> dba <person>`; and a certificate organization that is only the person's name, or `<business> (<person>)`, which is still read as a second name of the record after the applicant is renamed to its business, so that the person's name can reach the match keys, the stored row and, through a product or certificate name, the summary (in the real file of 2026-11-10 the three applicants with a mark all have an organization that carries the mark and is cut, so that no person's name survives). The list is context only, and no record of it raises a risk.
  - **Natural persons: PipeRoll.** A name comes from the operator cell, when the registry says that the operator is an enterprise or a startup (an individual is out by its category), and from the words of a title that have a lower-case letter followed later by a capital and no digit. Such a word is never a name when it has the shape of a surname with a prefix (`Mc`, `Mac`, `De`, `Van`, `Von`, `Fitz`, `St` and the like, then one or more capitalized parts, with or without one capital glued in front as the initial of a given name) or of an account handle (three or more lower-case letters before the first capital), whoever it names: no list of company names is kept, and a company that the operator cell names still has its key. A word that a letter touches (an accented letter, a letter of another script) is the stub of a longer word and is never a name, and only the part before a hyphen or an apostrophe gives a name. In a case caption the text before the first separator is the first party, a natural person in every caption of the real file, and it is dropped. The title of each of the newest entries that a record quotes goes through the same shapes: the first party of a caption is quoted as "A party" and each person-shaped part of a word as "[name]". A title that was cut is read, and quoted, without its last word, which may be the fragment of a name. Left open, quoted as written (and a key when the word has the brand shape of the title rule): a person in the second position of a caption; a person whose name has neither shape (an intercapped given name, a short lower-case run before a capital, a handle written with capitals, a lower-case letter or two capitals glued in front of a surname); the other part of a double-barrelled name of which one part only has a shape; a surname that apostrophes cut in the middle of its own parts; digits inside a word; a handle or a surname written wholly in letters that the ASCII reader does not see (accented letters, Cyrillic), which is neither quoted as a name nor a key; a caption written with another word (`against`, `&`, `versus`) or with no blank on a side; and the first party of a caption whose separator the cut of a title to 300 characters reached or went past. A product that is written like a handle (`macOS`, `openAI`) is quoted as "[name]" and gives no key.
  - **Natural persons: a residual of the kit.** The kit's English and Spanish shapes (`looksLikePerson` and `looksLikePersonEs`) read the ASCII hyphen, the ASCII apostrophe and the ASCII separators, so a name written with U+2010, U+2011, U+2212, the en dash, U+2018 or the middle dot U+00B7 is not read as a person by them. The FTC penalties connector maps every dash and quotation mark of Unicode to its ASCII form before it asks the rule (for the person shape only), and the Colombian connector maps the typographic apostrophes (U+2018 and U+2019); the other marks stay a residual of the Colombian rule and, for the middle dot, of the FTC rule. The FTC penalties workbook and the Colombian data of 2026-11-10 hold none of these characters. The remedy, if a list ever shows one, is to map the Unicode hyphens and quotation marks to ASCII before the rule, which fails closed.
  - **Record addresses are unique.** `WebSession.addRecords` drops a record whose address is already in the run's ledger, and the provenance and the verifier map a record by its address, so two records that shared one would lose the second. Most lists therefore build an address for each entity as a query on the page that describes the list, as `fda.ts` and `csl.ts` do (the last column of the table gives each address). Six lists whose first design gave every record the same page were changed to do so: `?tool=` for `charlotin`, `?developer=` for `fmti`, `?vendor=` for `omb_ai_inventory` and `canada_ai_register`, `?organisation=` for `epoch_ai` and `?applicant=` for `c2pa_conforming`. The GOV.UK lists and `piperoll` point at the newest item's own page and offer one record. `ftc_competition` points at the page of the newest matter and offers two, so that two parties of one matter share an address and the second record is dropped as a duplicate of the same matter, which is accepted.
  - **Pauses between requests, and robots.txt.** A list is read without `robots.txt`, as a file that its operator publishes for download (`connectorFetch` in `vrdb/connectors/index.ts`); the connectors keep the operators' Crawl-delay between their own requests through `connectors/kit/pause.ts` (`HOST_PAUSE_MS`): 20 seconds for `open.canada.ca` (`canada_ai_register`: the dataset record, then the file), 5 seconds for `www.ftc.gov` (`ftc_penalties`: `data.json`, then the workbook), 1 second for `www.datos.gov.co` (`co_sic_penalties`: the count, then the rows), 1 second for the bucket of the Commission's open data on Amazon S3 (`eu_dg_comp`: the antitrust file, then the Digital Markets Act file, with the pause once between them and never before the first request; the bucket gives no Crawl-delay, so the house pause of one second is used) and 400 milliseconds between two pages of the GOV.UK Search API (`govukSearchPages` in `connectors/govukSearch.ts`, at most three requests a second, for `uk_ofsi`, `uk_cma`, `uk_mhra` and `uk_atrs`). `oag.ca.gov` asks for 10 seconds between requests and gets one request a build. A connector that makes several requests is built by a factory that takes the sleep, as `fdaRecalls.ts` does, so that its tests do not wait; the pauses are timers and do not block the worker. The GOV.UK pager takes the pages as one search only when every page states the same total and the links of the results add up to it, and any failed page fails the download, so that a list taken in part, or shifted by a case published between two requests, never reaches the copy. Pages that disagree in a way only two requests can (a later page that states another total, or over two pages or more more or fewer distinct links than the total) are a search that moved between the requests: the download fails as unreachable (`fetch_failed`), which the bundler tries again (three attempts in all, 10 seconds apart), instead of as not the list, which would keep the previous bundle; a page that is no page of a search, or that begins elsewhere than it was asked, is still not the list. The bundler downloads the lists one after another, in the order of `VRDB_SOURCES`, which keeps the lists of one host apart.
  - **The NHTSA flat files are streamed.** `nhtsa_recalls` and `nhtsa_odi` each read one zip whose single entry is a tab-delimited text with no header row: 312.8 MB inflated and 247,176 lines for the recalls (29 fields), 390.6 MB and 154,460 lines for the investigations (11 fields), observed on 2026-11-10. `connectors/nhtsaFlat.ts` asks for the zip with one conditional request (at most 64 MiB for the recalls and 32 MiB for the investigations), checks its directory (exactly one deflated entry, no Zip64) and streams the entry through `streamZipEntry` of `vrdb/zip.ts` and the kit's `LineSplitter`, in pieces of 64 KiB, so that the entry is never held in memory; the size that the directory declares is checked against a cap before a byte is read, and the bytes are counted against it as they come. The reducer of each list keeps a small state for each campaign or investigation and then for each entity, and its result is one small JSON, which is what the kit's `read` gets: nothing that grows with the file runs synchronously in `read`. The lines (1,000,000), the bytes of a line (131,072) and the state are capped (one more than a cap is a `BodyTooLargeError`, never a shorter list), and the size and the CRC-32 of the entry are checked before anything is returned. The Part 573 texts and the manufacturer-supplied fields of the recalls, the summary of an investigation and the make, model and year of a vehicle are never decoded. The name of a filer or a manufacturer is capped at 80 characters once cleaned: a line whose name does not fit is never cut into an id or a key, and is counted as unreadable. The kit's allowance of unreadable lines is measured over the lines of the file, a hundredth of them (`allowanceBasis` of the read), and not over the entities, which would allow far fewer; beyond the allowance the parse fails, and a file in which more than one line in twenty has the wrong shape is no list at all and is refused as that. A line that names no entity (an empty cell, a placeholder, a name that the file itself retired) is counted apart from the lines that cannot be read. The years of a record are counted back from the newest report or opening day of the file and never from the clock, so that the same file gives the same body on any day.
  - **GLEIF** (Q53; `connectors/gleif.ts`, the one list of `financial_health`). A vendor with a stored LEI is asked by that LEI and by nothing else, never by name, since the completions of a name hold funds and subsidiaries beside the company and cost several requests within the 8 seconds of a run. The request is one, `lei-records?filter[lei]=<LEI>` on `api.gleif.org` (the filter answers 200 with a total of 0 for an LEI that GLEIF does not know, where the path form would answer 404, which the fetch turns into a failure), and the answer is read as hostile: a list of at most one entry whose total is that number and whose LEI is the one asked, or the lookup fails. An answer is kept 24 hours, and a spacer that all the workers share keeps the requests to 50 a minute (GLEIF allows 60 to each client). The record is reference data that the entity reports and its LEI issuer validates, a state dated by the registration's last update: it is no certification, and a lapsed registration means that the data were not renewed on time, not anything about the entity's conduct. The terms of Q53 bar implying that GLEIF provides, supports or is associated with VerifyWise or its products, so the second sentence of every record says that GLEIF is not associated with VerifyWise, it is never cut, and no text says that GLEIF endorses anything. The class is trusted press and the match is strong, by the LEI.
  - **What a run shows.** Every list the run asked is counted in the "Checked …" line and, when it held a record, named there; one that held none is named only behind **Show all lists** (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)); a list whose copy is out of date is named in a line of its own ("{list}: last updated {date}, not up to date") whenever the run asked it, with or without a record; every list that failed is named under "Not checked", so that a gap never reads as a clean screening; and the "Data sources" line credits one of the 22 only when it offered a record (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)). The server's briefing to the research loop does the same (`LISTS_NAMED_WHEN_FOUND` in `records/types.ts`). The outcome of each list that was asked carries the page that documents the list (`documentationUrl` of the list in `sources.ts`) as its address, in the trace and in the briefing.
  - **What the lists hold on the real files.** Measured on 2026-11-10 on saved copies of the real files, read with no network call, the lists hold these numbers of records (what they are made of is in brackets): `canada_sema` 1,486 (5,770 entries read), `california_breaches` 4,372 (5,425 rows, of which 6 are unreadable and skipped), `charlotin` 83 (2,286 decisions), `ftc_penalties` 293 (391 rows), `piperoll` 36 (110 entries), `eu_dg_comp` 2,166 undertakings, `disclose_io` 1,843 (2,433 rows from 1,870 organizations; 1,005 of the records carry a domain key; 2,853 keys in all), `uk_cma` 454 (2,593 cases), `canada_ai_register` 120 (412 rows), `ftc_competition` 201 parties (188 rows of the file), `omb_ai_inventory` 377 (3,611 rows), `co_sic_penalties` 2,218 (3,938 rows), `uk_atrs` 125 (155 results; 43 with a Companies House number; 118 on the same capture since the lines of an address are no suppliers, 2026-11-12), `nhtsa_recalls` 1,616 (247,176 lines, 15,382 campaigns), `fmti` 11 (13 developer columns and 100 indicator rows), `uk_mhra` 2,036 (7,643 notices from 624 lists, 7 of them with an unclear day), `nl_algoritmeregister` 248 (1,638 rows), `nhtsa_odi` 583 (154,460 lines and 5,357 investigations; 4 manufacturers that are bare acronyms get no record), `fedramp` 558 (656 products and 560 providers; 424 of the records have a domain key), `epoch_ai` 381 (3,708 models), `c2pa_conforming` 199 (322 products) and `uk_ofsi` 19 (19 notices among 249 documents; 30 keys). No list has an error, and the California list is the only one with unreadable rows (6). `gleif` was read on 5 real answers: 3 give a record, one is an unknown LEI that gives none, and one is the answer of the path form, which is refused as unsupported.
- **The CVE records** (Q7, open since 2026-10-31). The CVE Program publishes the records that changed on a day as an asset of a release of `CVEProject/cvelistV5` on GitHub (`cve_<day>_at_end_of_day`, a zip of one JSON file per record). The bundler asks the GitHub API for the release of each day after the previous bundle's cursor up to yesterday (UTC), at most seven a request, oldest first, until no day is left, and downloads each delta from the project's releases on `github.com` only. A day before yesterday that has no end-of-day release (cvelistV5 has had three since 2025: 2025-01-13, 2026-06-20 and 2026-07-19) is read from its last hourly release: the connector asks `git/matching-refs/tags/cve_<day>_`, takes the greatest hour among the tags `cve_<day>_HH00Z`, and reads that release, whose changes are cumulative; the day is noted as read up to `HH00Z`, since the changes of the last hour may be missing. It is refused as `not_found` only when the day has no hourly release, or when that release cannot be found, and a release that exists without its delta stays `feed_without_file`; a missing release for yesterday ends the days read, because it may not be out yet. A record leaves by a status of its own (`REJECTED`), not by its absence (a rejected record names no product, so its stored copy keeps the keys it was matched by; a published record whose affected vendors are all `n/a` or missing gets no key, and its stored copy loses the keys it had), and the copy dates a rejection by the record's own `dateRejected` (the time of the build when the record states none it can read), so a CVE loaded months after its rejection is not said to be rejected on the load day. The copy keeps the records published in the last 24 months whose highest CVSS base score is 9.0 or more and every rejected record: on the baseline of 2026-10-31 that is 15,994 + 18,859 = 34,853 rows of 417,486 records, and the bundle of 2026-11-23 holds 36,067.
  - **A record that is read and left out is retired.** A published record that a day (or the baseline) leaves out because it is no longer critical, or has aged out of the window, is dropped from the bundle, and the bundler drops the published records older than the 24-month window at each build. The load retires every id the copy holds that the bundle lacks: the store takes its keys off the stored copy and invalidates its content hash, so it is never offered again and is written afresh if it is critical again. Its status is kept, except that a rejection the list publishes again as a published record becomes `active`, dated by the bundle's `builtAt`; that is counted as a change of status, so the status job runs for it and a pending finding that cited the rejected CVE is labelled again. A plain retirement is no change of status and relabels nothing: a finding that cites the record keeps its label. An entry that could not be read is not retired, because its state is unknown.
  - **A bad record is left out and counted.** A day applies the records it can read. An entry whose JSON does not parse, or that `readCveRecord` calls an error, is left out and counted (`skipped`). A day fails as `parse_errors`, and the CVE list keeps its previous bundle, only when its unreadable entries number more than 10 and more than 1% of its record entries (`cveUnreadableAllowed(entries) = max(10, floor(entries / 100))`): that is a new format and not an odd record. An entry over the 8 MiB limit of the zip still fails its day.
  - **The baseline.** The first CVE bundle, and one that starts over, is built from a baseline file of the CVE Program: `npm run vrdb:bundle -- --cve-baseline <day>_all_CVEs_at_midnight.zip` (see [Refreshing the lists for a release](#refreshing-the-lists-for-a-release)). The file is `<day>_all_CVEs_at_midnight.zip` of the newest day at https://github.com/CVEProject/cvelistV5/releases (about 650 MB; GitHub serves it as `.zip.zip`, and the bundler takes both names), and reading it needs about 720 MB of temporary disk. The bundler keeps what a day keeps, sets the cursor to the day before the file's and then rolls forward by the daily changes. It refuses a file that is not named as a baseline is, one that holds no CVE record, and one with more unreadable entries than `cveUnreadableAllowed` allows over all its entries. Without `--cve-baseline` it rolls the previous bundle forward from its cursor, and with neither it refuses the list (`no previous bundle to roll forward`). A deployment never reads a baseline: it loads the CVE bundle like the others, and the records the bundle lacks are retired, so each load makes the copy match the bundle. **Building from a newer baseline is the remedy** for a day the bundler refuses, and it makes the bundle exact again.
  - **The notice.** The CVE Terms of Use let anyone copy the records on one condition: every copy reproduces MITRE's copyright designation and the licence, and they set no commercial-use or field-of-use limit (trademarks are outside the grant). Both are stored in the `cve` row of `vrdb_sources` (`licence` and `attribution`, which every record of the copy references by `source_id`): the licence is `CVE Terms of Use (https://www.cve.org/Legal/TermsOfUse). CVE Usage: ` and the CVE Usage paragraph of the Terms word for word (411 characters, `CVE_TERMS_LICENCE`, pinned by a hash), and the attribution is `CVE records Copyright © 1999-<year>, The MITRE Corporation, under the CVE Terms of Use (https://www.cve.org/Legal/TermsOfUse)`, with the current UTC year, read at each use. The screen shows the attribution in the Data sources line of the coverage, and the user guide page of vendor risks (`shared/user-guide-content/content/risk-management/vendor-risks.ts`) reproduces the paragraph. Each record's text keeps its lead "CVE Program (cve.org)." The OFAC row carries its Q5 note and the UN row its Q6 note the same way.
- **The changes of 2026-11-12** (the second final audit of the branch). Each changed connector moved to a new version, so that a copy holding an earlier reading of its list takes the new one; `csl` moved to version 3, `uk_ofsi` to version 3, then 4, then 5, then 6, then 7, then 8, then 9, then 10 and then 11 and `uk_atrs` to version 4, then 5, then 6, then 7, then 8, then 9 and then 10 (below). Where a rule of these two lists cannot tell, it is meant to lean to giving nothing rather than a guess: a doubtful Companies House number is withheld rather than given to the wrong firm, a part that may be a person is left out rather than kept as a party, and a part that is no name of its own gives no record, since a wrong key is worse than none. These rules read names out of free text by heuristics, and the shapes that they misread have a long tail of invented inputs (a frozen corpus of such inputs, `ukOfsiAtrs.corpus.test.ts`, pins how many of its rows move to each class against the readings of `uk_ofsi` 7 and `uk_atrs` 8). The privacy rule of `uk_ofsi` (no person's name in a key) and the one-firm rule of `uk_atrs` (no number beside a second firm) are best effort, not complete: the families of shapes named below as open are the known ones where a person's name still reaches a key, a key of generic words is still published, or a number is still given beside a second firm. Elsewhere a doubtful case gives no key, no record or no number, the safe direction. The residuals below, and the shapes the person rule does not read (spec D2.23, the connector header), are the shapes where the rules do not yet do so.
  - **A change of the key rules reaches the stored records only when a list is parsed again.** The keys of a record, and for some lists its id, are made when its list is parsed and are stored with it in `vrdb_records`, while the vendor's own keys are made at each run. So a new legal form (`identity.ts`) or a new shared host (`sharedHosts.ts`) applies to the vendor at once and to a copy only when its list is parsed again, that is when a new bundle is built and loaded. A copy loaded before the change keeps the old keys of a list until then; the connectors whose records the change alters moved to a new version (the slash forms: `uk_cma`, `uk_mhra`, `eu_dg_comp`, `c2pa_conforming`, `eu_fsf`, `fda_ai_devices` and `canada_ai_register`; `csl` for its own names). The bundler parses every list afresh at each build, and the load applies every bundle whose hash differs from the last one loaded, so a new build carries a change of the key rules to every deployment that loads it. The lists whose output the change leaves as it was keep their version: `california_breaches`, `canada_sema`, `charlotin`, `co_sic_penalties`, `disclose_io`, `epoch_ai`, `fedramp`, `fmti`, `ftc_competition`, `ftc_penalties`, `kev`, `nl_algoritmeregister`, `ofac`, `omb_ai_inventory`, `nhtsa_odi` and `nhtsa_recalls` gave the same ids, keys and texts before and after on their real files; `uk_sanctions` and `un_sanctions` were compared on their fixtures only, and `aiid` has no real file to compare. `uk_ofsi` gave the same ids, keys and texts on its real file too, and moved to version 3, then 4, then 5, then 6, then 7, then 8, then 9, then 10 and then 11, for its party rule (below). The read-through sources (`gleif`, `sec`, `nvd`) have no version, and whether their stored answers follow a change of the key rules before they expire was not checked.
  - **Legal forms written with a slash.** `identity.ts` knows A/S, I/S, K/S, P/S, N/V and S/A as legal forms, and `shortName` takes one off the end of a name (`Danspin A/S` becomes `Danspin`, as `Danspin AS` does), so each such name gets a second key without the form. The ids of `uk_cma`, `uk_mhra` and `eu_dg_comp` are those of the name without the form, so `Coloplast` and `Coloplast A/S` are one MHRA record. A form that is not at the end (`A/S Norske Shell`, `Origio A/S (2760)`) is left alone. Versions: `uk_cma` 3, `uk_mhra` 3, `eu_dg_comp` 2, `c2pa_conforming` 2, `eu_fsf` 5, `fda_ai_devices` 2 (the second key without the form; 3 of the 640 records of the real list gain one) and `canada_ai_register` 2 (the second key, and the vendor id is that of the name without the form; 1 of the 120 records of the real register moves, `Milestone Systems A/S`). In the OFSI notices a party that ends in a slash form ends the company, as `Ltd` does, so a person after it (`Danspin A/S and Casey Sample`, `Danspin A/S's director Casey Sample`) is left out; the real notices hold no slash form, so the slash forms alone did not move the version of `uk_ofsi` (its party rule did, below). A residual: the Dutch register splits a provider cell at a slash (`SEPARATORS` in `nlAlgoritmeregister.ts`), so `Danspin A/S` is read as the provider `Danspin A`; the real register holds no slash form today.
  - **MHRA headings.** A block that begins with `Response form` or `Reply form` (with `Customer` or `Distributor` before it or not) or with `Customer letter` is no heading. When the first heading of a segment has a head (the text before its first semicolon) with fewer than three letters or more digits than letters, such as a model code (`04S68-01`) or a notice number (`FA-2015-6`), the entry gives no row and no later block takes its place.
  - **ATLAS text.** `atlas.ts` takes the characters nobody sees out and cuts each run of combining marks to `MARK_RUN_MAX` (16; `boundMarkRuns` of `kit/clean.ts`) before the NFKC fold, as the kit's lines do. The records of the real file are unchanged (`atlas` 3).
  - **Days in the future.** The bound of the NHTSA flat files (`latestPlausibleDay` of `nhtsaFlat.ts`: a week after the day of the download or the parse, `FUTURE_DAYS_TOLERATED`) also covers the crash reports, where an incident month after it makes a report unreadable and a submission month after it is read as none (`nhtsa_sgo` 2), the FDA recalls, where a product initiated after it is left out and counted as skipped (`fda_device_recalls` 3), and PipeRoll, where an entry whose first day is after it is unreadable (`piperoll` 2).
  - **CSL names.** A name is also keyed without the State lists' successor clause (`, and any successor, sub-unit, or subsidiary thereof`), without a bracket at its end, and by a capitals alias in that bracket, but not in an all-capitals name, not a legal form and not a second bracket (`cslNamesOf`). The id stays that of the listed name. `csl` 3: on the real list 201 of the 5,169 records gain 388 name keys, which a copy parsed at version 2 would otherwise lack.
  - **OFSI parties** (`uk_ofsi` 11). After a word of an organisation, only a connective of a name means that the company's name goes on. A connective is a word of the closed list `NAME_CONNECTIVES` in `ukOfsi.ts` (`of`, `for`, `the`, `de`, `du`, `del`, `di`, `da`, `al`, `el`, `van`, `von`, `der` and the others), a word that a hyphen joins to the next only after `al` or `el` (`HYPHEN_CONNECTIVES`: `al-Kovex`), or one that an apostrophe joins to the next only after an elided `d` or `l` (`ELIDED_CONNECTIVES`: `d'Italia`, `l'Union`); a word with a dot is never one. Any hyphen (any of `\p{Pd}`, U+2010 and U+2011 among them, and the minus sign U+2212, which `foldedOf` turns into one) and any apostrophe (`'`, U+2019, U+02BC; `APOSTROPHES`) count the same (`isNameConnective`, `JOINED_WORD`), and so they do where `al` or `el` leads a person's name (`ARTICLE_NAME`: `Casey al-Sample` is a person whatever hyphen joins the two, U+FE63 and the minus sign U+2212 among them). Any other word in lower case, such as a legal form that no list holds (`s.r.o.`, `sp. z o.o.`, `d.o.o.`, `a.s.`, `e.V.`, `eood`) or a compound such as `e-Solutions`, `e-commerce` or `do-it`, no longer carries the name on, so a person after it is a party of their own and is left out. A part that holds a word of an organisation and ends in a dotted form in lower case (`Bank of Kovex s.r.o.`, `DOTTED_FORM`) ends the name too. A status in brackets is `in`, at most two more words and `liquidation`, `administration` or `receivership` (`STATUS_WORDS`: `(in liquidation)`, `(in voluntary liquidation)`, `(in administration)`, `(in receivership)`), read in any case (`(In Liquidation)`, `(IN ADMINISTRATION)`). It is never a person. When a title is split into a list, a part loses a status at its end (`withoutStatus`), before or after a trailing alias in brackets (`Kovex Trading Ltd (in liquidation) (KTL) and Casey Sample` gives the party `Kovex Trading Ltd`), so `Kovex Trading Ltd (in liquidation) and Casey Sample` gives the record `ofsi:kovextrading` with the keys of the company's name. A status ends the name of a company: a list of persons and parts that end in a status is split whatever the order, and the persons are left out (`Kovex (In Liquidation) and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex`, `Casey Sample and Kovex Trading Ltd (In Liquidation)` gives `Kovex Trading Ltd`). Since version 6 a status ends a name only on a part that has a name of its own (`continuesName`). A part that, without its status and its alias, is legal forms alone or opens with a word of `FIRM_TAIL_WORDS` (son, sons, daughter, daughters, brother, brothers, bros, sister, sisters, associate, associates, partner, partners, company, co, cie and family) goes on with the name before it: its status counts as no company and never splits the list, so the title reads as the same firm without a status does (since version 7 the list is split when a status has already ended a name before such a part, below). `J Sample & Co (in liquidation)`, `Casey Sample & Co (in liquidation)` and `Casey Sample and Sons Ltd (in liquidation)` are each one party, the whole title with its status, as a title that names a company alone (the record `ofsi:jsamplecoinliquidation`), and `Casey Sample & Sons Trading Ltd (in liquidation)`, `Casey Sample & Partners Holdings Ltd (in liquidation)` and `Alden Brook & Co Ltd (in liquidation)` are one firm the same way. This is the reading chosen for a firm named after a person: its whole name, with the words that have the shape of a person's name, is its key, its title and its link, as it was before the status was read; leaving the person out would leave the firm with no record. A part that opens with any other word, a word of an organisation included, is no tail: the person is left out, and the part is a company of its own when it has a distinctive word (`Casey Sample and Kovex Shipping Ltd (in liquidation)` gives `Kovex Shipping Ltd`). Since version 8 a part of generic words alone gives no record, so `Casey Sample and International Shipping Ltd (in liquidation)` and `Casey Sample & Trading Services Ltd (in liquidation)` give none (they gave `International Shipping Ltd` and `Trading Services Ltd` before; the final gate of version 8, below). A person's name that a status ends is a party of its own and is left out, and what follows it is another party: `Casey Sample (In Liquidation) and Kovex Trading Ltd`, whatever the case of the status, and `Alden Brook (In Liquidation)'s Kovex Trading Ltd` (`splitAtPossessives` cuts at a possessive after a status) give only `Kovex Trading Ltd`, and the notice shows its kind alone as its title and the collection page as its link. A status in brackets on its own, set apart by a comma, a semicolon, a slash or an ampersand (`Kovex Trading Ltd, (in liquidation) and Casey Sample`), is no party and no person (`STATUS_PART`, `isAside`), in a title and in the list of a description; a description is not split at a semicolon, so there `Kovex Trading Ltd; (in liquidation) and Casey Sample` keeps the party `Kovex Trading Ltd; (in liquidation)`. A title that names the company alone keeps the status in its party and its keys (`Kovex Trading Ltd (In Liquidation)`). The list of a description is split at a comma and at the word and (`describedCellsOf`), and a party it gives that way keeps its status (`Kovex Trading Ltd (In Liquidation) and Casey Sample` gives the party `Kovex Trading Ltd (In Liquidation)` there, and the person is left out); a part of it that is split again as a title's slot is (`cellsOfSlot`: at an ampersand, a slash or a possessive) loses its status as a title's part does. A possessive right after bracket groups ends the company when the words before the groups end in a legal form or a word of an organisation (`Kovex Trading Ltd (EBL)'s director Casey Sample` and `Kovex Trading Ltd (in liquidation)'s director Casey Sample` give `Kovex Trading Ltd`; `endBeforeBrackets`). Residuals that a test pins: `Bank of X and Casey Sample` and `Bank al-Kovex and Casey Sample` stay one party, and so does a status without brackets (`Kovex Bank in administration and Casey Sample`); since version 8 `Bank of Kovex eood and Casey Sample` gives `Bank of Kovex eood`, the person left out, since `eood` is a legal form of a list of the file (`endsAFirmName`); connectives of other languages that the list lacks (such as `voor` and `im`) and the hyphen heads `as-`, `an-` and `at-` end the company, which leaves the person after them out. Residuals that a test pins: the list of a description is split at the word and before a part is read, so there `Example Bread and Sons Ltd (in liquidation)`, `Casey Sample and Sons Ltd (in liquidation)` and `Casey Sample and Partners (in administration)` give no party since version 7 (before, `Sons Ltd (in liquidation)` or `Partners (in administration)` alone): the part before the word and has the shape of a person's name, and the tail part after it is left out with the person whose firm it names, the side of privacy; `Casey Sample (in bankruptcy) and Kovex Ltd` stays one party in a title, the person with it, and gives the parties `in bankruptcy` and `Kovex Ltd` in the list of a description, since bankruptcy is no word of `STATUS_WORDS`; a person before a bracket and a possessive (`Casey Sample (Kovex)'s director Alden Brook`) stays in the party; a word in lower case after the article (`Kovex Trading Ltd and Casey al-sample`, the hyphen written as U+2010) is no word of a name, so the title stays one party and the list of a description keeps `Casey al-sample` as a party of its own. Version 7. A status in brackets tells nothing of the words before it: every rule reads a part without its status (`withoutStatus`), so `Kovex & Co (in liquidation)'s director Casey Sample` gives `Kovex & Co` and leaves the director out, as `Kovex & Co's director Casey Sample` does. A part that ends in a status or in a word of `FIRM_TAIL_WORDS` (`Kovex & Sons`, `Kovex & Cie`) ends a firm's name, so a person after it is a party of their own and is left out (`Kovex & Sons and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex & Sons`). When a title or a description is split into a list (`firmsOfCuts`), a part that goes on with the name before it (`continuesName`) is never a party of its own when it has no name of its own (`hasNoNameOfItsOwn`): every word of it, with the status and any alias in brackets set aside, is a tail word, a word of an organisation, a legal form or a connective (`Co Ltd`, `Sons Ltd`, `Partners Holdings Ltd`). Such a part is joined to the name before it (`Kovex & Sons Ltd and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex & Sons Ltd`, key `kovexsons`); it is left out after a name that a legal form or a status already ends (`Kovex Ltd and Co Ltd and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex Ltd`, and so does `Kovex Ltd (in liquidation) & Co and Casey Sample` in a title); and it is left out with the person whose firm it names (`Casey Sample (in liquidation) and Sons Ltd and Kovex Ltd` gives `Kovex Ltd` alone). After ` & `, a part that opens with a word of an organisation and has no name of its own joins a name that is not yet ended (`Svarog Shipping & Trading Company Limited and Casey Sample` gives the one company, key `svarogshippingtrading`). Version 8 joined after the word and the same way; version 9 no longer does (below), so `Svarog Shipping and Trading Company Limited and Casey Sample` gives `Svarog Shipping` alone, key `svarogshipping`, as at version 7, and no record `ofsi:trading` (the residual AND_FIRM). A part with a name of its own stays a party (`Kovex Ltd and Company Watch Ltd` gives `Kovex Ltd` and `Company Watch Ltd` in the list of a description, `Kovex Ltd and Bank of Valletta and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex Ltd` and `Bank of Valletta`). A legal form of the rule's own list on its own is no party and is never joined, so its notice stays unreadable (`Kovex Ltd, Ltd and Casey Sample`). Persons, parts that end in a status and tail parts are read as a list once a status has ended a name (the count of the parts that go on, `continuing`): `Casey Sample (in liquidation) & Co Ltd` gives no party, the person and the person's firm both left out, while `Casey Sample & Co (in liquidation)`, whose status ends no person's name, stays one firm. Since version 8 no record is made of a tail alone (`sons`, `co`, `trading`, `pty ltd`) that a cut of a list gave, by the final gate below (since version 9 a party that is the whole slot is not gated, below) (at version 7 a firm cut at the word and still gave `ofsi:trading`): such a key would match vendors that the notice does not name, and a wrong key is worse than none. Residuals of version 7 that a test pins: `Kovex & Son's director Casey Sample` stays one party, the person with it, since a possessive right after a tail word does not end the firm; `Kovex Ltd and Partners Group AG and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex Ltd` alone, since generic words alone after a name that has ended read as a tail. Not pinned, and new in version 7: for the same reason the list of a description `Kovex Ltd and Partners Group AG`, with no person in it, gives `Kovex Ltd` alone, so a company named by generic words alone is lost there (version 6 kept it); since version 8 such a company gets no record where a list cuts it, and since version 9 it keeps its record as the whole slot (below). Not pinned, and as at version 6: `Kovex & Sons's director Casey Sample` stays one party with the person too; in a title, `Anne-Marie Dupont, Brothers (in liquidation)'s Kovex` and `Casey Sample, Sons (in liquidation)'s Kovex Ltd` stay one party with the person, and so do the firms named after a person that a company follows (`Casey Sample and Associates (in liquidation) and Kovex Ltd`, `Casey Sample, Example Bread & Sons Ltd`), as the pinned `Casey Sample & Co (in liquidation) and Alden Brook Ltd` does, and `Casey Son's Kovex Ltd`; the list of a description is not split at an ampersand, so there `Kovex Ltd & Alden Brook Ltd and Casey Sample` gives the one party `Kovex Ltd & Alden Brook Ltd`. In the list of a description `Kovex and Co` is now one party, as in a title (key `kovexand`; it was `kovex`). Version 8. One test decides whether a part ends a firm's name, on every path (`endsAFirmName`, which `endsOrganisation`, `splitAtPossessives`, `ampersandGoesOn` and `firmsOfCuts` read): a status in brackets, a last word that is a legal form of any list of the file (`isFormWord`: the person rule's own, the forms of the keys, `LAST_WORD_FORMS`, `MORE_FIRM_FORMS`, a dotted form in lower case, and `AS` or `A.S.` but not the word `as`), `Sdn Bhd` as the last two words, or an unknown form of two to five capitals after a name that is no person's (`CAPITALS_FORM`: `Kovex Trading ZRT and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex Trading ZRT`). So the list of a description `Kovex s.r.o. and Partners Group AG` gives `Kovex s.r.o.`, and `Kovex Trading FZE and Partners Group AG and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex Trading FZE`. After a name that has not ended, a part of generic words alone that opens with a word of an organisation joins it after ` & ` (`ampersandGoesOn`; version 8 joined it after ` and ` too, version 9 no longer does); after a name that has ended, such a part gives no key (`Kovex Ltd and Trading Ltd and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex Ltd`; it gave `Trading Ltd` too), except the form of a German partnership after ` & ` (`partnershipGoesOn`: `Kovex GmbH & Co. KG and Casey Sample` gives `Kovex GmbH & Co. KG`, key `kovexgmbhcokg`). The side persons of a part are put aside before the part is read (`leadOf`, and `besidesAName` in `cellsOfSlot`): `Svarog Shipping & Trading Company Limited (Casey Sample, director)` gives the one company and leaves the director out (with ` and ` in place of ` & `, `Svarog Shipping` alone since version 9), and `Zentrix Limited f/k/a Casey Sample; Kovex Shipping SE` gives `Zentrix Limited` and `Kovex Shipping SE` (before, the person stayed in the key of the whole slot). A tail that has the shape of a person's name is read as a tail only after a part that is no person's and when its last word is a legal form written in capitals or with a dot (`isShapedTailOfAFirm`): `Halvex and Sons FZE` is one firm, while `Kovex Trading Ltd and Son Le` gives `Kovex Trading Ltd` and leaves the person out. Last, one gate on every key that a cut of the slot gave (`hasDistinctiveWord` in `ofsiReadingOf`; since version 9 a party that is the whole slot is not gated, below): a party whose every word is generic gets no record. A word is generic (`isGenericWord`) when it is a word of `FIRM_TAIL_WORDS` (which now holds `Söhne`, `Fils`, `Figli`, `Sønner`, `Zonen`, `Hijos` and `Filhos` too), a word of an organisation, a legal form of any list, a word of a status, an alias in brackets or a mark, or a connective, a word of a role or a joiner of a title written in lower case; with a capital such a word is a word of a name (`Le`, `Van`, `Plus`), so `Van Shipping Ltd` keeps its key. `Sons Ltd`, `Co Ltd`, `Trading Company Limited` and `Partners JSC` cut from a list never become a record. The cost, accepted: a real firm whose whole name is generic words (`Partners Group AG`, `International Bank`) has no record either where a list names it beside another party (at version 8 it had none as the whole slot either; version 9 gives it back, below). The lists of the person rule (`isPersonShaped`, `LEGAL_FORMS`, `LAST_WORD_FORMS`, `consistsOfLegalForms`) and `isLegalFormsOnly` stay narrow on purpose, so that a person is never read as a company because of a rarer form. Residuals of version 8 that a test pins: a firm named after a person keeps the person's name in its one party (`Casey Sample & Sons`); a person first in a title's list stays in one party (`Casey Sample & Example Ltd`); a spaced dash in a title joins a company and a person (`Kovex Ltd - Casey Sample`); and a firm whose only form is `AS` is read as a person (`Alden Brook AS's director Quilla Quarrin` gives no party: the possessive cuts at `AS`, and both sides go). Not pinned: a firm whose only form is one that the person rule does not list is read as a person when its name has the shape of one (`Kovex FZE` gives no party, and `Kovex FZE and Casey Sample` none), the side of privacy. Version 9. The rules of version 8 are narrowed back where they moved a firm's key away from the key of version 7, and some shapes are added (the rows of `ukOfsi.wave13.test.ts`). A side person of a sole trader's marker or of a bracket is put aside only when the firm comes first (`besidesAName` reads the first side): `Casey Quarby d/b/a Svarog Shipping & Trading Company Limited` gives `svarogshippingtrading` again (version 8 gave `svarogshipping`), the person left out, and `Zentrix Limited f/k/a Casey Sample; Kovex Shipping SE` still gives `zentrix` and `kovexshippingse`. A part of generic words alone joins the name before it only after ` & ` (`firmsOfCuts`); after ` and ` it is a cut part of its own, which the gate drops: the list of a description `Standard Chartered Bank and Trust Bank` gives `standardcharteredbank`, while a title of it with no person, read as one slot, gives `standardcharteredbankandtrustbank`. The gate of generic words reads the words of the key's name (`hasDistinctiveWord(shortName(party))`: a leading `The` is no word of it), so `Kovex Ltd and The Trading Company Ltd and Casey Sample` gives `kovex` alone (version 8 also gave `trading`). It is applied only to a part that a cut of the slot gave: a party that is the whole slot (the title's slot, or the list of a description that is one cell) and holds a capital letter is the name that the notice gives and is not gated (`oneParty` in `ofsiReadingOf`), so `SAS AB`, `AB Bank Limited`, `EV Group`, `Partners Group AG` and `The Partners Group AG` keep their key, and so do a whole title `Sons Ltd` (record `ofsi:sons`) and `International Bank`; a slot in lower case alone is the words of a role and is gated (the title `its director, the owner` gives no party). A tail of the shape of a person's name after a firm whose name has ended (`Söhne FZE`, `Sons FZCO`) makes the slot a list again, so that the persons before the firm do not reach its key: `Casey Sample & Kovex Management & Söhne FZE` gives `kovexmanagementsöhnefze` and leaves the person out (version 8 kept the person in the one key). More legal forms end a firm's name (`MORE_FIRM_FORMS`: `CJSC`, `ZAO`, `OAO`, `PAO`, `AO`, `TOO`, `ApS`, `WLL`, `Ltda` and `NV/SA`; `Kovex & Partners CJSC and Casey Quarby` gives `kovexpartnerscjsc`); they are no forms of the person rule. A last word of capitals joined by a hyphen is a legal form of a shaped tail (`isShapedTailOfAFirm`: `Kovrin Holdings & Sons FZ-LLC` is one firm). A given name whose second part a hyphen joins in lower case is a word of a person's name (`HYPHENED_GIVEN_NAME` in `isNameWord`: `Ji-sung Park` and `Abdul-rahman Haddad` are persons, `Kovex Ltd's director Ban Ki-moon` gives `kovex`), while `Coca-cola Ltd` and `Kovex Co-op` stay firms. A part that is legal forms alone once its alias is put aside closes the name before it, so `Kovexbank, N.A. (KNA) and Casey Quarby` is unreadable, the safe side (version 8 read the title as one party that held the person's name). Residuals of version 9 that a test pins, where the rule gives nothing: `Svarog Shipping and Trading Company Limited and Casey Sample` gives `Svarog Shipping` alone (AND_FIRM), and a firm of generic words alone after ` and ` has no record (`Metro Bank and Trust Company Ltd and Casey Sample` gives `metrobank`); a firm named by a form and a generic word gets no record as a cut part of a list (BRAND_IS_FORM_IN_A_LIST: `SAS Group and Casey Sample` and `AB Bank Limited and Casey Sample` give no party); a brand of a name, a hyphen and a word in lower case, with no form, is read as a person and dropped (HYPHEN_LOWER_BRAND: `Coca-cola Hellenic`). Open residuals, where a person's name or a generic word still reaches a key: a status in brackets before a sole trader's marker hides the side person from the title's list reading, so the title `Kovex Ltd (in liquidation) f/k/a Casey Quarby and Zeltara Ltd` gives one key that holds the person's name (STATUS_BEFORE_MARKER, pinned, as at version 7; the description gives `kovex` and `zeltara` and leaves the person out); a person first in a title's list stays in its one key (PERSON_FIRST_IN_TITLE, pinned in the property test of `ukOfsi.test.ts`: `Casey Ao and Kovex Ltd` gives `caseyaoandkovex` in a title); notices that version 7 could not read and version 9 reads can hold the joiners that the person rule does not read (`via`, `or` and the others of the connector header), where the person stays in the party; and a title whose one party is generic words alone is not gated (WHOLE_SLOT_GENERIC, as at version 7, pinned in `ukOfsi.wave14.test.ts` since version 10: `Sons Ltd` gives `ofsi:sons`, and a title whose party is `& Co Ltd` gives a record whose id is `ofsi:` with the key `name:coltd`). On the frozen corpus (2,644 rows of `uk_ofsi` at version 11), 21 rows have a key that holds a word of a person's name that the keys of version 7 did not hold (the class `person token new` of `ukOfsiAtrs.corpus.test.ts`); versions 9 and 10 give the same 21 rows, on these rows and on the 2,596 rows of the corpus of wave 14, so no version since 9 added one. Version 10. Two rules of version 9 are narrowed back to the reading of version 7 (the rows of `ukOfsi.wave14.test.ts`). First, the side person of a part (in a bracket or after a sole trader's marker) makes the slot a list only where the part may be read on its own (`sideReadable` in `cellsOfSlot`): when it is the first part that names anyone, when it follows a part that holds a person, when no cut at ` & ` or ` and ` stands right before it, or when its first side is the tail of a firm (`isTailOfAFirm`). So a firm whose name holds ` & ` and that has a side person of its own keeps its whole key, its side person put aside and counted: the titles `Ernst & Young LLP (Casey Sample, director)`, `Marks & Spencer Group plc (formerly Casey Sample)`, `Smith & Nephew plc (Casey Sample, director)` and `Kovex & Zentrix Ltd d/b/a Casey Sample` give `ernstyoung`, `marksspencergroup`, `smithnephew` and `kovexzentrix` again (version 9 cut each at ` & `). The rows of version 9 stay: `Casey Quarby d/b/a Svarog Shipping & Trading Company Limited` gives `svarogshippingtrading`, `Zentrix Limited f/k/a Casey Sample; Kovex Shipping SE` gives `zentrix` and `kovexshippingse`, and `Torvik & Sons AB f/k/a Casey Sample vs Kovex Ltd` gives `torviksons` and `kovex`. A firm with ` & ` beside a person who is a party of their own is still cut, as at version 7 (`Ernst & Young LLP and Casey Sample` gives `ernst` and `young` in a title, and `ernstyoung` in the list of a description), and so is one after a name that has ended (`Kovex Ltd and Marks & Spencer Group plc (formerly Casey Sample)` gives `kovex`, `marks` and `spencergroup`); both are still so at version 11, which keeps the ` & ` of a firm only when the firm is listed after a firm with a side person (below). Second, a given name that a hyphen joins in lower case (`HYPHENED_GIVEN_NAME`) is no word of a person's name when the name ends in a legal form of any list (`isFormWord`) or in `Oyj` or `Lda` (`HYPHEN_RULE_FORMS`, read by this rule only, so `Kovex Oyj` is still read as a person), so `Agro-trade Kovex FZE`, `Hi-tech SE`, `Bio-tech Kovex SE` and `Agro-trade Kovex Oyj` keep their key again, while `Ji-sung Park` and the director of `Kovex Ltd's director Ban Ki-moon` are still left out. HYPHEN_LOWER_BRAND above therefore holds as written: only a brand with no legal form (`Coca-cola Hellenic`) is read as a person. Residuals of version 10 that `ukOfsi.wave14.test.ts` pins, all open: a hyphened given name before a surname spelled as a legal form is read as a firm, so the person's name is in the key, as at version 7 (HYPHEN_GIVEN_FORM_SURNAME: `Ji-sung Ao`, `Ji-sung Se`, `Ji-sung Park AS` and `Ji-sung Park Co` give `jisungao`, `jisungse` and `jisungpark`, and the title `Casey Sample and Ji-sung Ao` gives `caseysampleandjisungao`; version 9 left them out); after a name that a legal form ends, a firm whose name holds ` & ` and whose tail has the shape of a person's name (`Spencer Ltda`) is cut at ` & ` in a title, as version 7 already cut it after `Kovex Ltd` (AMPERSAND_FIRM_UNKNOWN_FORM, since version 8, whose forms end more names: `Bank of Ostrava DMCC and Marks & Spencer Ltda` gives `bankofostravadmcc` and `marks`, and `Petro-chem KG and Marks & Spencer DMCC (In Administration)` gives `petrochemkg` and `marks`; the list of a description keeps the firm whole, `marksspencerltda` in the first), and for the same reason `Procter & Gamble PAO; Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample)` gives `procter` and `kovex`, since `Gamble PAO` is read as a person; a firm after a firm whose side person is put aside is glued to it in a title (TWO_FIRMS_GLUED, as at version 7: `Ernst & Young LLP (Casey Sample, director) and Kovex Ltd` gives `ernstyoungllpandkovex`); and a shaped tail after a name that has not ended keeps the slot whole (PERSON_FIRST_IN_TITLE and TWO_FIRMS_GLUED: `Kovex & Sons FZE (in liquidation) and Zentrix Ltd` gives `kovexsonsfzeinliquidationandzentrix`, and `Casey Sample and Torvik & Sons EOOD (in liquidation) and Bank of Ostrava OOO` gives one key that holds the person's name, as the same titles with `Ltd` do at every version; version 7 split these two only because it knew no `FZE` or `EOOD`). Version 11. Where a title is read as a list because a firm in it has a side person (in a bracket, `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director)` or `Kovex Ltd (formerly Casey Sample)`, or after a sole trader's marker, `Kovex Ltd f/k/a Casey Sample`), and every person of the title is such a side person, the ` & ` inside the name of a firm listed after the first such firm is kept as a part of that name (`sideAt` in `cellsOfSlot`, read by `firmsOfCuts`; the rows of `ukOfsi.wave15.test.ts`), and so it is in a part of the list of a description that is read again as a title's slot. A firm listed before it may come first: `Zentrix Ltd and Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Ernst & Young LLP` gives `zentrix`, `kovex` and `ernstyoung`. A title whose firm with ` & ` comes first with its own side person is not read as a list and stays one glued key (TWO_FIRMS_GLUED above: `Ernst & Young LLP (Casey Sample, director) and Tate & Lyle PLC` gives `ernstyoungllpandtatelyle`). `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Ernst & Young LLP` gives `kovex` and `ernstyoung`, the person put aside and counted (version 10 gave `kovex`, `ernst` and `young`, and version 7 the one key `kovexltdandernstyoung`); `Kovex Ltd (formerly Casey Sample); Tate & Lyle PLC` gives `kovex` and `tatelyle`; and `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Marks & Spencer Ltd and Zentrix Ltd` gives `kovex`, `marksspencer` and `zentrix`. The ` & ` is kept only when the part before it is the first part of its firm, holds no person and no status, and does not end in a legal form, a word of an organisation or a word of `FIRM_TAIL_WORDS`; otherwise it is a cut, as at version 10: after `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and`, the firms `Zentrix Ltd & Alden Brook Ltd`, `Zentrix Bank & Alden Ltd` and `Kovex Trading & Alden Ltd` give `zentrix` and `aldenbrook`, `zentrixbank` and `alden`, and `kovextrading` and `alden`, and `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) & Zentrix Ltd` gives `kovex` and `zentrix`. Only the first ` & ` of the firm is kept, so the title `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Marks & Spencer & Zentrix Ltd` gives `kovex`, `marksspencer` and `zentrix` (the description gives `marksspencerzentrix`, as before). The rule also moves titles that version 7 already read as a list and cut at every ` & ` (a status on the firm with ` & `, a third part, a side person of the second firm's own, or ` & ` between the two firms): the second firm keeps its whole key in the title now, so `Zentrix Trading Limited (director Casey Sample), Procter & Gamble Inc (In Administration); Ernst & Young BV` gives `zentrixtrading`, `proctergamble` and `ernstyoung` (versions 7 and 10 gave `procter`, `gamble`, `ernst` and `young`). Of the 51 rows of the hunt of wave 14 that the corpus pins under AMPERSAND_FIRM_UNKNOWN_FORM (`ukOfsiAtrs.corpus.test.ts`), 6 were of this family instead, a firm with a side person first and then `Ernst & Young`, `Procter & Gamble`, `Kestrin & Halvex Trading` or `Johnson & Johnson`, and now give the second firm's whole key (`Coca-cola OAO (Dr Jane Brook) and Kestrin & Halvex Trading PLC` gives `cocacolaoao` and `kestrinhalvextrading`, where version 10 gave `kestrin` and `halvextrading`); the residual stands for the others, as written above. Residuals of version 11 that the rows of `ukOfsi.wave15.test.ts` or of the corpus pin: ` and ` inside the name of the firm after the side person is still a cut, in a title and in a description (`Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Morgan and Wexley Ltd` gives `kovex`, `morgan` and `wexley`); a firm with ` & ` listed before the firm with the side person is still cut in a title, as at version 7 (`Ernst & Young LLP and Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director)` gives `ernst`, `young` and `kovex`; the description gives `ernstyoung`); a person who is a part of their own turns the rule off, so `Casey Sample and Kovex Ltd (Jane Doe, director) and Ernst & Young LLP` gives `kovex`, `ernst` and `young` with 2 persons in a title, as at version 10, and so does `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Ernst & Young LLP and Casey Sample`, as at version 7 (the description gives `ernstyoung` in both); and the title `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Procter & Gamble Co` gives `kovex` and `procter` with 2 persons, as at version 7, since `Gamble Co` is read as a person (the description gives `proctergamble`). Open, and pinned row by row by the corpus (`ofsiNow` in `ukOfsiAtrsCorpus.r11.json`, a row of the source `rvp77`): the part before the ` & ` may hold a person whom the person rule does not read (after a joiner such as `plus` or `+`), and the firm after it is then joined to that part, so in `Hexacore Holdings S.r.l. acting on behalf of José McThistlewood plus its director Jean-Luc bin Brannagh + José van der Velasquez & Kovrin Services Corporation` the firm loses its own key `kovrinservices` (version 10) to one key that holds the persons' names and its own; version 7 could not read that title. The same happens when such a person comes after the firm, behind a spaced dash (`SPACED_DASH_IN_TITLE`): `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Ernst & Young LLP - Casey Quarby` gives `kovex` and `ernstyoungllpcaseyquarby` in a title (version 10 gave `kovex`, `ernst` and `youngllpcaseyquarby`, and version 7 one key for the whole slot; the description gives `kovex` and `ernstyoung`). Open, named TWO_FORMLESS_FIRMS_GLUED (the rows of `ukOfsi.wave15.test.ts`): in a list that has a side person, a ` & ` between two parts that hold no legal form is read as the inside of one firm's name, where versions 7 and 10 cut it into two keys, so `Zentrix Ltd, Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director), Barclays & HSBC` gives `zentrix`, `kovex` and `barclayshsbc` (versions 7 and 10 gave `barclays` and `hsbc`) and `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director), Alden Ltd (in liquidation), Vitol & Glencore AG` gives `vitolglencore`; no person token and no other firm's key reaches a record, but two firms lose their own keys, which is a loss of recall. Open as well: the rule is off when the firm with ` & ` has no legal form and a side person of its own, so `Kovex Ltd (Casey Sample, director) and Marks & Spencer (Jane Brook, director)` gives `kovex`, `marks` and `spencer` with 2 persons in a title (version 10 the same, version 7 one glued key). Version 4 changed the hyphen, apostrophe and status rules; version 5 the case of a status, a status on its own or before an alias, a status that ends a company, a possessive after brackets and the hyphens after `al` and `el` in a person's name; version 6 the status of a part that goes on with a firm's name and the status that ends a person's name; version 7 what a status tells of the words before it, the end of a firm's name at a tail word, and the parts of a split list that go on with the name before them; version 8 the one test of the end of a firm's name, the join after the word and, the side persons of a tail and the gate of generic words; version 9 the narrowing of version 8 back where it moved a firm's key (the side person before a marker, the join after the word and, the gate of a whole slot), the words of the key in the gate, more forms, a tail with a hyphen and a given name with a hyphen; version 10 the side person of a part after a cut at ` & ` or ` and ` and the hyphened given name before a legal form, both back to the reading of version 7; version 11 the ` & ` inside the name of a firm listed after a firm with a side person. On the real list of 2026-11-10 (19 records) the output is the same as at versions 2 to 10.
  - **UK ATRS** (`uk_atrs` 10): the ampersand, the address lines and the one-firm rule of the Companies House number (above). Version 5 (`mayNameAFirm` in `ukAtrs.ts`, which reads `mentionsOf` since version 6): a part of the supplier cell that the accept rule refuses still counts as another firm, and so withholds the number from the one supplier, unless it is details of the supplier. It does not count when it is a web address or a host (`www.x.co.uk`, `x.co.uk`, `https://x.example`); when it is a label of details followed by details that name no firm (the text before its first colon that no `//` follows is made of words of `LABEL_WORDS` alone, such as Contact, Tel, Email, Website, Web site, Address or More information, or is one character: `Contact: sales team`); or when it is a clause in lower case that names no firm (`, a company registered in England`): no later word with a capital other than the countries of the United Kingdom and Companies House (`WORDS_OF_NO_FIRM`), and no closing legal form other than `company`, `co`, `as` or `the` (`clauseNamesAFirm`). A clause or a label that names a firm counts (`supplied by Quillon Data Ltd`, `quillon data ltd`, `with Quillon Data`, `Partner: quillon data`), and any other text before a colon is read as a name. So does a name before its details (`Quillon Data Ltd: www.quillon.example`) and a firm in brackets after details (`www.quillon.example (Quillon Data Ltd)`), unless that name is a supplier of the cell. Residuals that a test pins: `with quillon data` (lower case, no legal form) gives the number, since it reads like `Contact: sales team`; `registered office: 1 Example Street` withholds it; a name whose details after the colon are an e-mail address gives it, by the older `@` rule (`Quillon Data Ltd: sales@quillon.example`). Version 6 (`mentionsOf`): a web address anywhere in a refused part (`WEB_TOKEN`: a scheme and `//`, or `www.`, as a word of the text) splits it, and every name around it is details, which count unless a supplier of the cell has that name; the piece before the first address names no one when it is a label or a clause in lower case that names no firm (version 7 narrows this, below). So, beside the supplier Zorbex Analytics Ltd, `Zorbex Analytics Ltd - https://zorbex.co.uk`, `Website https://zorbex.co.uk`, `See https://zorbex.co.uk` and `Visit https://zorbex.co.uk` give the number, and `www.zorbex.co.uk - Quillon Data Ltd`, `www.quillon.example (in partnership with Quillon Data Ltd)` and `https://quillon.example (via Quillon Data)` withhold it. `See` and `Visit` are words of `LABEL_WORDS`, and a label may end in a dot (`Tel.: 020 7946 0000`). `WEB_ADDRESS` (a web address, a host or a bare domain that names no one) must be the whole text. The bracket at the end of a part that does not count as a name of its own is read too: a refused part, the details of a supplier, and a part that names no one (a telephone number, a country, an e-mail address, the deploying body). A firm in it withholds the number unless it is a supplier (`020 7946 0000 (Quillon Data Ltd)`, `Email: sales@quillon.example (Quillon Data Ltd)`; `England (Zorbex Analytics Ltd)` gives it). Residuals of version 6 that a test pins: `Zorbex Analytics Ltd: www.zorbex.co.uk (ZAL)` and `www.zorbex.co.uk (a product of Zorbex Analytics Ltd)` withhold the number, and so do `Company number: 12345678` and `Zorbex: www.zorbex.co.uk`, whose heads are no label words; `quillon - www.quillon.example` gives it; `More information: https://zorbex.co.uk/about` withholds it, since the cell is split at a slash between two word characters (`SEPARATORS`) and the piece after it reads as a firm. A part refused for prose or for half a bracket still counts, and so do `Subcontractor: Quillon Data`, `iProov` and a one-word name in lower case. Version 7 (`mentionsAroundWebAddresses`): a piece beside a web address names no one only in a shape that no firm's name has: joiners alone (`and`, `or`, `and/or` or `&`, in any case: `JOINER_ALONE`), a label (words of `LABEL_WORDS`, in any piece), a host in lower case (`quillon.ai https://quillon.ai`), a clause in lower case of two words or more that names no firm and has no colon after it, on either side of an address (`LOWER_CASE_CLAUSE` and `clauseNamesAFirm`, read by `mentionsOf` for a piece after an address: `https://quillon.example (a company registered in England and Wales)` and `https://zorbex.co.uk or call 020 7946 0000` give the number), or, before the first address and with no colon after it, words that begin with a letter in lower case, have no capital anywhere and name no firm, one word included (`through www.quillon.example` gives the number). After an address one word in lower case names a firm (`https://zorbex.co.uk and quillon` withholds the number). The other pieces name a firm, and the number is withheld from the one supplier: a head before a colon that is no label (`HEAD_BEFORE_DETAILS`: `quillon data: https://quillon.example`, `eBay: www.ebay.co.uk`; a head in which nothing else names a firm is itself the name when it has a letter, so `020 7946 0000: https://zorbex.co.uk` gives the number), a word with a capital anywhere (`CAPITALISED`: `iProov https://www.iproov.com`, and in a clause too, `clauseNamesAFirm`: `with iProov`, `supplied by eBay`, `www.zorbex.co.uk (powered by eBay)`), and words that begin with a digit or a mark (`6point6 - https://6point6.co.uk`, `8x8 https://8x8.com`, `(quillon) https://quillon.example`). The word `and`, `or` or `&` in lower case that joins a piece to the address beside it is no name (`JOINER_BEFORE`, `JOINER_AFTER`; a name that opens with `And` in capitals keeps it, so `And Zorbex Ltd - https://zorbex.co.uk` gives the number to the supplier And Zorbex Ltd). So two addresses of the one supplier joined by `and`, `or`, `and/or` or `&` give the number, and `https://zorbex.co.uk and quillon` withholds it, on the safe side. A wrong or doubtful number is withheld rather than given to the wrong firm. Residuals of version 7 that a test pins: `with quillon data` (no capital, no legal form) and `quillon - www.quillon.example` still give the number, and so does `Link - https://link.example`, whose word before the address is read as a label. A residual that no test pins: a name in lower case with no legal form after an address reads as such a clause (`https://quillon.example via quillon data` gives the number, as `with quillon data` does). Version 8 (`mentionsAroundWebAddresses`): a name shaped like a host with a capital anywhere, next to a web address (`Quillon.ai https://quillon.ai`, `QUILLON.AI https://quillon.ai`, `(Quillon.ai) https://quillon.ai`), names a firm, as `iProov` does, unless a supplier of the cell has that name (`Zorbex.io Ltd` beside `Zorbex.io - https://zorbex.io` or `ZORBEX.IO https://zorbex.io` gives the number); a host in lower case is an address. Residuals of version 8 that a test pins: the supplier's own host written with a capital (`Zorbex.co.uk https://zorbex.co.uk` or `Zorbex.ai https://zorbex.ai` beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd) withholds the number, and so does a word with a capital inside in a clause in lower case (`a provider of eLearning software`, `providing iOS apps`, `developer of the eConsult tool`), as `with iProov` does; `a provider of learning software` gives it. Version 9 (`hostNamesAFirm`, read by `mentionsOf`): one test of a host that names a firm in every position, a line of its own, a bracket at the end of a part that is no name of its own, the rest after a label and a piece beside a web address alike: the text has the shape of a real host (`HOST`: labels joined by dots whose last label is letters alone, two or more), a capital anywhere and a label that no public host has (`PUBLIC_HOST_LABELS`). So `iQuillon.ai` and `quillon.AI` on a line of their own, and `https://zorbex.co.uk (iQuillon.ai)`, withhold the number, and the special case of version 8 beside a web address is gone. This narrows the rule of version 5 that a refused host names no one: a host in lower case still names no one. A host is the supplier's own when its first label is the whole compact short name of a supplier of the cell (`isOwnDetail`: `Zorbex.co.uk` beside Zorbex Ltd gives the number, on a line of its own too), so `Digital.ai` beside Digital Health Ltd withholds it, and so does `Website: Zorbex.co.uk` beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd. A text with the shape of a bare domain whose last label holds a digit, or whose labels are all those of a public host, is a detail (`isDottedDetail`: `Tel.02079460000`, `No.12345678`, `Est.2015`, `V2.1`, `GOV.UK`, `NHS.UK`), and so is a label with digits alone (`isDetailsOnly`: `Phone 020 7946 0000`; since version 10 six digits or more); neither names anyone or is a supplier, so the number is given beside them (they withheld it before), and `U.K.` is a country. Initials (`I.B.M`, `E.Y.`) still name a firm. A clause in lower case whose last word, once the details after it are put aside (`isDetailWord`: a telephone number, a word of a label), is a legal form names a firm (`clauseNamesAFirm`: `quillon data ltd 020 7946 0001` withholds the number; since version 10 the details put aside are a trailing run that holds a word with no letter).
