Residuals of version 9 that a test pins: a head before a colon that is no label names someone whatever it is (`HEAD_BEFORE_COLON`: `Tel.02079460000: 020 7946 0000` withholds the number); one word in lower case before the first address names no one (`LOWER_WORD_BEFORE_ADDRESS`: `quillon https://quillon.example` gives it); a comma inside a bracket cuts it, and half a bracket counts (`COMMA_SPLITS_A_BRACKET`: `020 7946 0000 (registered in England, 020 7946 0001)` withholds it); and a host whose first label is only a part of the supplier's name withholds it (`OWN_HOST_SHORTER_THAN_NAME`: `Zorbex.co.uk` beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd). Words joined by dots with a capital are read as a name (`Cyber.Essentials` withholds the number). The residuals that withhold the number are on the safe side. Version 10. The capital test of a clause in lower case reads every word again, a label word included, as at version 8 (`clauseNamesAFirm`: `supplied by Link`, `with Info` and `via Contact` beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd withhold the number; version 9 gave it), and only a trailing run of details that holds a word with no letter (a telephone number) is put aside before its last word is read (`quillon data ltd tel 020 7946 0001` withholds the number). Details alone need six digits or more (`DETAIL_DIGITS_MIN` in `isDetailsOnly`): `Phone 020 7946 0000` and `Tel 020 7946 0000` are details, while `Link 360` and `Info 24` are names again (`Link 360` alone is a supplier with its number; beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd it is a second firm, and the number is withheld). A bracket at the end of a part that is a name of its own, or a part that is a bracket alone, names a second firm and withholds the number when it is a host written as a name, or has two words or more and ends in such a host or in a legal form of the keys that a clause does not end in (`bracketNamesAFirm`: `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (Quillon Data Ltd)`, `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (via Quillon Data Ltd)`, `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (iQuillon.ai)`, `(Quillon Data Ltd)` on a line of its own; version 9 gave the number). An alias without a form (`Amazon Web Services (AWS)`, `PriceWaterhouseCoopers LLP (PwC)`), the supplier's other name (`OWN_OTHER_NAME`: `trading as`, `formerly`, `previously`, `known as`, `also known as`; `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (trading as Zorbex Ltd)`) and the supplier's own name, when its compact short name is the supplier's (`isOwnDetail`: `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (Zorbex Analytics Limited)`), keep it. `t/a` is no such marker in a cell: the cell is split at the slash first (`SEPARATORS`), so `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (t/a Zorbex Ltd)` gives no record at all, as at version 8 (pinned in `ukAtrs.test.ts`). Residuals of version 10 that a test pins: a label word with a capital in a clause in lower case names a firm, so `registered in England Tel 020 7946 0000` beside the one supplier withholds the number (CAPITAL_LABEL_IN_CLAUSE, the safe side; with `tel` in lower case it gives it); a bracket with a plain name of words alone is not read (BRACKET_PLAIN_NAME: `Zorbex Analytics Ltd (Quillon Data)` gives the number); one accepted name that a joiner makes of two firms is one supplier (JOINED_IN_ONE_NAME: `Mavro Systems Ltd with Q2 Analytics Ltd` gives the number to `mavrosystemsltdwithq2analytics`). These two are open: they give the number beside a second firm. So do the residuals that `ukAtrs.test.ts` pins since then (the named residuals of the number, under the ids RV13A-1 to RV13A-5): a firm in lower case that a run of label words ends (RV13A-3, as at version 8: `quillon data ltd tel` and `supplied by quillon data ltd contact` beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd give the number, since a run of label words alone is put aside nowhere and its last word is no legal form; `with Link` withholds it and `with link` gives it); a dotted word whose last label holds a digit, and label words with six digits or more, which are details (RV13A-4, since version 9: `Channel.4`, `Studio.24` and `Contact 365 247` beside Zorbex Analytics Ltd give the number; version 8 withheld it); and a firm in a bracket that a second bracket follows, since the bracket is read from the first `(` to the last `)` (RV13A-5, as at version 8: `Zorbex Ltd (Quillon Data Ltd) (UK)` gives the number). On the safe side, the supplier's own other name written as an acronym or a shorter name with a legal form reads as a second firm and withholds the number (RV13A-1: `SSCL (Shared Services Connected Ltd)`, `Conversations By Ami Ltd (Ami Ltd)`, `Tessaly AI (UK) Ltd (previously Tessaly AI Limited)`, where the bracket read runs from the first `(` so `previously` does not open it; the review that found it counted 2 such lines in 100 lines of the real list, both beside another firm, so the real records are unchanged), and so does a product name of the shape of a host in a bracket (RV13A-2: `Mavro Systems Ltd (Node.js)`, and `ASP.NET` or `Vue.js` the same way). The supplier's own host in a bracket after its name, when the host's first label is only a part of that name (`Zorbex Analytics Ltd (Zorbex.co.uk)`), withholds the number since version 10, the safe side (as OWN_HOST_SHORTER_THAN_NAME). The residuals of version 9 above still hold. On the real list (118 entities) the output is the same as at versions 3 to 9. The precision was measured at 29 of 30 accepted names when the list was built. On the whole list of November 2026 (155 records, 118 entities, read by hand), 15 entities are prose fragments and 7 more name a product or a public body, so 96 to 103 of the 118 are suppliers (81 to 87 %).
  - **CVE records** (`cve` 3). `sourceUpdatedAt` is stored as a UTC instant: a time without a zone is UTC, and a bare day stays a day. On the incremental path a record keeps the reading of the bundle that first held it, so a row that an earlier baseline wrote from a process in another time zone is corrected only by a bundle built from a new baseline or by the record's next update.
- **The status job** (`vrdb/relabel.ts`, spec D5.7 cases 10 and 11, Q16). A load or a read-through answer that changed the status of a record adds the job `vrdb_relabel` of its list (see [automations](../infrastructure/automations.md#vendor-risk-database-queue)). A completed research run adds the status job of every list whose records its saved findings cite, after the commit and without waiting for it (`relabelCitedLists` in `pipeline.ts`): the run was offered the records that were active when it started, and the job reads today's statuses, so it relabels a finding whose record changed while the run was going and changes nothing otherwise. It adds no run, and a failure to add it is logged. Organization by organization (it lists their ids and reads the findings of one at a time, every statement filtered on `organization_id`), it finds the pending findings with a label that cite a record of the list whose status is no longer the one they stored (an entry that left its list, a CVE rejected on any list, an entry back on its list), decides each label again by the rule of the run from the sources its basis describes with the statuses of today, and writes it in the finding's own organization while the finding is still pending and its basis is the one the job read (three passes at most). The job is deduplicated per list (`deduplication` with `keepLastIfActive`, id `vrdb_relabel_<list>`, no fixed job id): an add while the list's job waits is dropped, since that job reads the findings when it starts; an add while it runs is kept as the next job of the list, which BullMQ queues when the running one ends, so a change written after the running job read the findings is not lost. Each changed record shows its status from the day it changed, and `rejected_by` names the list that rejected its CVE when that list is another ("Low: the CVE Program rejected the record on …"). A finding a person accepted or dismissed is never changed, and nobody is notified.
- **The hosts nobody reviewed.** `npx ts-node scripts/vendorResearchUnknownHosts.ts [--days N] [--limit N]` (90 days and 50 hosts by default, at most 3,650 and 500) counts, over the completed runs of every organization, the runs and the organizations that cited each host the source registry does not know, from the list that the trace of each run keeps, so that the maintainer can review the registry. It only reads, and prints host names and counts, never an organization, a vendor or an address; a failure prints the error's name, never its message.
- **The coverage of a run.** A run stores `coverage` (`RunCoverage`) and `registry_version`: for each list and read-through source the run covers (a list the release ships no bundle of has no row) its outcome (`found`, `none`, `failed`, `off` or `not_researched`), its mode (`mirror` or `read_through`), whether it was asked, the records found by a strong and by a weak match, the day of the copy and whether it is stale; for a list that attests something in the vendor's favour (`attests: true` in `vrdb/sources.ts`: the FedRAMP Marketplace and the C2PA Conforming Products List) that matched, `attestations`, one per offered record, in closed words (`CoverageAttestation`: the best status the record names, FedRAMP's four or a C2PA conformance, the FedRAMP impact level of that offering, the day of the status or of the newest conformance, the number of offerings or products, the strength of the match and the day of the copy), read back from the text the list's own connector wrote (`vrdb/attestations.ts`, `fedrampAttestationOf`, `c2paAttestationOf`); a status the text does not give is null, never guessed; for each web database its outcome; the data source's name and attribution for each list, as the licence of the list asks (the CVE Program's line is `CVE records Copyright © 1999-<year>, The MITRE Corporation, under the CVE Terms of Use`, shown in the Data sources line, and the client's own `ATTRIBUTIONS.cve` is the same string), and how many distinct sources the findings cite in each category (`cited`). The trace keeps the cited categories and up to 50 hosts that the registry does not know (`TraceRecorder.cited`). The findings section shows the coverage under the latest run (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)).

### Refreshing the lists for a release

The lists are refreshed by a maintainer before a release, from a checkout with network access. No deployment downloads them.

```bash
cd Servers
npm run vrdb:bundle                                   # every list the gates allow
npm run vrdb:bundle -- --only kev,ofac                # some lists
npm run vrdb:bundle -- --allow-shrink uk_ofsi         # accept a list that shrank, after a look at why
npm run vrdb:bundle -- --cve-baseline <day>_all_CVEs_at_midnight.zip
```

`scripts/vrdbBundle.ts` downloads each active list (`activeMirrors`, so a list behind a closed gate is skipped) through its connector (`CONNECTORS` in `vrdb/connectors/index.ts`) and `connectorFetch`, which goes through the page guard, parses it, and writes its bundle and its entry in `manifest.json`. It sends no validators, so every list is read in full. A list keeps its previous bundle, and the script prints the reason, when:

- the document is not the list (`wrong_root`) or parsed with errors (`parse_errors`);
- it holds no record (`empty`);
- it holds more than a fifth fewer records than the previous bundle (`SHRINK_LIMIT`, 0.2), unless its id is given to `--allow-shrink`. A bundle built by a new connector version is not held to this check, since a new reading may have fewer records by design.

Entries that a connector skipped are printed with their count. A download that fails with a fetch error is tried three times in all, 10 seconds apart (`ATTEMPTS`); a refusal of what was read is not tried again. A failure is printed as a fixed reason and the list's id, never an error message: the address of the EU file carries a token (Q2), which the EU connector reads from the operator's RSS feed and keeps in a local variable only (`feed_without_file` when the feed names no such file), and the connector also checks that the feed answered from `https://webgate.ec.europa.eu` with no port (`isOperatorAddress` in `connectors/eu.ts`) and the file from that host and the path of the XML 1.1 file (`isFileAddress`). The script writes every list it could, prints how many were bundled and which failed, and exits 1 when any list failed (2 for an unknown option).

The CVE records are read by their changes. Without `--cve-baseline`, the script rolls the previous bundle forward a day at a time from its cursor with the incremental connector, until no day is left, and drops the published records older than the 24-month window. The first time, or to start over, it needs the CVE Program's baseline file (see **The CVE records** above).

Commit everything under `Servers/data/vrdb/` with the release: the changed `<id>.json.gz` files and `manifest.json`, whose diff shows which lists changed and their counts. Each build writes a new `builtAt`, so a rebuilt list has new bytes even when its records are the same, and each deployment loads it again at its next start.

On 2026-11-23 the script bundled 31 lists. `eu_dg_comp` failed: the Commission's open-data files no longer carry `caseInitiationDate`, so the connector refuses them as not the list, and the release ships no bundle of it until the connector is updated. The same day the Epoch AI connector was changed to read the first row of a model that the list names twice and count the repeat as unreadable, since the list named two models twice in November 2026.

## Vendor Scorecard

The scorecard assesses vendor risk across dimensions:

| Dimension | Purpose | Weight |
|-----------|---------|--------|
| Data Sensitivity | Type of data vendor accesses | High |
| Business Criticality | Impact if vendor fails | High |
| Past Issues | Historical incident severity | Medium |
| Regulatory Exposure | Compliance requirements | Medium |

### Risk Score

The server computes `risk_score` on every vendor write, from the scorecard
answers as they will be stored (`calculateVendorRiskScore`,
`utils/vendorScorecard.utils.ts`); a `risk_score` in the request is ignored.
Each answer is normalized against its scale's highest value and weighted
(sensitivity 0.3, criticality 0.3, past issues 0.2, regulatory exposure 0.2);
the sum × 100, rounded, is the score (0–100). A vendor with no answer has
`risk_score` NULL and the vendor table shows "Not assessed", where it used to
show a green 0%. The drawer's live preview uses the same table
(`Clients/src/domain/utils/vendorScorecard.utils.ts`).

### Vendor risk level

`vendorrisks.risk_level` is computed by the server on create and on every
update from likelihood and severity as they will be stored, with the formula
project risks use (`calculateRiskLevel` in `utils/riskLevel.utils.ts`:
likelihood + 3 × severity → "No risk", "Low risk", "Medium risk", "High risk",
"Very high risk"). A `risk_level` in the request is ignored, so a level can't
go stale when likelihood or severity changes. The Risks tab cards, card filter
and level filter group levels through `vendorRiskLevelLabel`
(`Clients/src/application/utils/riskLevel.ts`): "No risk" is the lowest card,
and an older stored "Very low" counts there.

Migration `20261122200000-vendor-risk-level-and-score-recompute` recomputed
every stored level (rows without likelihood or severity kept theirs) and every
score (NULL where no answer is set).

## Vendor-Project Relationship

Vendors are linked to projects through a many-to-many relationship:

```
Vendor ─────┬───── Project A
            ├───── Project B
            └───── Project C
```

### Link Management

A vendor PATCH writes links only when it sends `projects`; a field-only save
leaves them as they are, and `projects: []` unlinks every use case the user can
see. Vendors are visible to the whole organization, but a non-Admin sees only
some use cases, so `setVisibleVendorProjects` (`utils/vendor.utils.ts`) adds
and removes only links to use cases the user can see (`getUserProjects`); links
to the others are kept, and an id the user can't see is ignored. An Editor who
isn't on every linked use case can therefore save the vendor, where the old
delete-visible-then-reinsert-all hit the primary key and answered 500. The
response's `projects` is read back from `vendors_projects` after the write.

The vendor form fetches the use cases each time it opens. New links are
picked from approved use cases only, but a link the vendor already has to a
use case pending approval or rejected stays selected, named with its status
("Beta (Pending approval)", "Gamma (Rejected)"), and is sent back on save, so
it goes only when the user removes it. Ids the form doesn't list (use cases
the user can't see) are sent back unchanged too.

### Deleting a vendor

A vendor delete is a hard delete (`deleteVendorByIdQuery`,
`utils/vendor.utils.ts`), in one transaction:

- the vendor row is locked first; an unknown id answers 404 and touches nothing
- its risks are deleted, active or not, with their custom field values;
  framework and risk links cascade
- its custom field values, notes (`attached_to = 'VENDOR'`), task links, share
  links and use case links are removed
- pending approval requests for it are withdrawn
- the vendor, and each risk that was still active, get a "deleted" history
  entry. A risk deleted earlier already has one.

The deletion is recorded as made by the user who asked for it, in the console
or through the Advisor. A deletion with no known user is recorded as made by
the system.

## Vendor Risk Management

A vendor risk has no status or lifecycle column: it is active or soft-deleted
(see [Soft Delete](#soft-delete)).

### Risk Properties

| Property | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| risk_description | What the risk is (required on create) |
| impact_description | How it affects the organization |
| likelihood | `Rare` … `Almost certain` (required on create) |
| risk_severity | `Negligible` … `Catastrophic` (required on create) |
| risk_level | Computed by the server from likelihood and severity (see [Vendor risk level](#vendor-risk-level)) |
| action_plan | Mitigation strategy |
| action_owner | Responsible user, a member of the organization |

Removing a user from an organization sets `action_owner` to NULL on that
organization's vendor risks they own (and `assignee` / `reviewer` on its
vendors), in the removal's transaction (`clearOrganizationReferencesToUser`,
`Servers/utils/user.utils.ts`). Other organizations' rows are untouched. No
vendor risk change history is written for this clear, as for the other
columns it clears.

### Soft Delete

Deleting a vendor risk is a soft delete (`deleteVendorRiskByIdQuery`):
- `is_deleted` set to true, `deleted_at` recorded
- a "deleted" history entry (Vendor Risk: Active → Deleted)
- custom field values and framework links are kept, so a restore brings the
  risk back whole
- hidden from the default lists, reports and risk link suggestions

A deleted risk shows in the Risks tab under the "Deleted" status filter. It
opens read-only, titled "Deleted risk", without the Linked risks tab. Holders
of `vendor.edit` can restore it from its row menu or from the modal.
`PATCH /v1/vendor-risks/:id/restore` clears the flag, records a history entry
(Deleted → Active) and queues a link recompute. It is gone for good only when
its vendor is deleted.

### Linked risks

A saved vendor risk has a **Linked risks** tab in its edit modal
(`components/LinkedRisksPanel/VendorRiskLinksPanel.tsx`). It has two groups:

- **Child risks**: the project risks that inherit from the vendor risk —
  value-chain inheritance, described in
  [Risk Management](./risk-management.md#value-chain-inheritance). A vendor
  risk is only ever a parent. **Link a project risk** opens a picker that ranks
  the project risks in the vendor's projects first, with a "Same project" chip.
- **Related vendor risks**: other vendor risks that describe the same
  exposure, at this vendor or another one (`related_to`, see
  [Related vendor risks](#related-vendor-risks)). Each row carries the other
  vendor risk's vendor as a chip. **Relate a vendor risk** opens a picker over
  all active vendor risks, this vendor's first.

Suggested children come from the hierarchy pass. An Admin can start it from a
project risk's panel, or from this tab with **Suggest children**
(`POST /api/riskLinks/vendor-risks/:vendorRiskId/suggest-hierarchy`), which runs
the same pass on only the clusters of related risks that contain a project risk
in the vendor's use cases. The tab then polls for the results for a bounded
window, like the project panel. Suggestions are confirmed or dismissed here with
the same actions and dismissal reasons as on the project side (inheritance
reasons for a child, related reasons for a related vendor risk). When the
vendor risk's `risk_level` changes, each child is flagged "Parent level
changed" on its own panel; the vendor tab does not show that flag.

Reads use `GET /api/riskLinks/vendor-risks/:vendorRiskId` (both groups; a
related row's `relatedRisk.vendorName` is only set here) and
`.../shared-projects`; writes go through the existing `POST /api/riskLinks` and
`PATCH /api/riskLinks/:id`.

**Who sees the link actions.** The tab gates on the keys the routes authorize
(`routes/riskLinks.route.ts`, defaults in `config/rolePermissions.config.ts`),
through `useCan` and the client's `BUILTIN_UI_PERMISSIONS`:

| UI | Route | Key | Built-in roles |
|----|-------|-----|----------------|
| **Link a project risk**, **Relate a vendor risk** and their forms | `POST /api/riskLinks` | `riskLinks.edit` | Admin, Editor |
| Row actions: Confirm, Dismiss (with its reason form), Remove link, Restore | `PATCH /api/riskLinks/:id` | `riskLinks.review` | Admin, Editor, Reviewer |
| **Suggest children**, **Scan for related vendor risks** | `POST .../suggest-hierarchy`, `POST .../vendor-risks/recompute` | `riskLinks.admin` | Admin, SuperAdmin |

Actions a user may not take are hidden, not disabled, as the vendor tables hide
row actions without `vendor.edit`. An Auditor sees the links, their reasons
and the Show dismissed toggle, and no actions; the empty state's "Link a
project risk…" hint shows only with `riskLinks.edit`. A custom role follows its
loaded permissions. The project risk panel (`LinkedRisksPanel/index.tsx`)
applies the same keys to **Link a risk** and the row actions, and shows **Mark
reviewed** on a "Parent level changed" flag only with `riskLinks.edit` (the
acknowledge route's key); the flag itself shows to everyone.

### Related vendor risks

Two vendor risks can be related, never parent and child. The row is a
`risk_links` row with `source_vendor_risk_id` and `target_vendor_risk_id` set,
`relation_type = 'related_to'`, and the smaller id as the source, so a pair has
exactly one row (see [Risk Management](./risk-management.md#vendor-risk-pairs)
for the constraints).

Scoring (`services/riskLinks/vendorRelated.ts`) needs similar wording first:
Jaccard over description + impact words, stopwords dropped, at least two
shared words. Without it nothing else counts.

| Signal | Weight | Rule |
|--------|--------|------|
| `similar_wording` | 3 or 2 | Jaccard ≥ 0.3 scores 3, ≥ 0.15 scores 2; detail names up to 6 shared words |
| `same_vendor` | 1 | Both risks belong to one vendor (a risk with no vendor never matches) |
| `shared_framework` | 1 | Both are mapped to a common framework; detail names them |
| `shared_use_case` | 1 | Two different vendors serve a common use case; detail names them |

A pair at `LINK_SCORE_THRESHOLD` (3) or above is suggested, at most
`MAX_LINKS_PER_RISK` (20) per vendor risk. Recompute only prunes derived
suggestions that fell below the threshold; a confirmed or dismissed pair keeps
its status and only has its score refreshed.

Recompute runs on the `vendor_risk_link_recompute` BullMQ job (jobId
`vendor-risk-link:{org}:{vendorRiskId}`), enqueued after a vendor risk is
created or updated. When a vendor's use cases are added or removed, its active
risks are rescored in one `vendor_risk_link_recompute_batch` job (jobId
`vendor-risk-link-batch:{org}:vendor-{vendorId}`) that reads the org's vendor
risk scoring rows once. Existing organisations are backfilled with **Scan for
related vendor risks** on an empty Linked risks tab, or directly:
`POST /api/riskLinks/vendor-risks/recompute` (Admin, SuperAdmin), which
enqueues one batch for every active vendor risk and returns `{ enqueued }`
(the number of vendor risks). A vendor risk that fails inside a batch is
re-queued as its own `vendor_risk_link_recompute` job.

`POST /api/riskLinks` creates a pair by hand with
`{ sourceVendorRiskId, targetVendorRiskId, relationType: "related_to" }`. Any
other relation type is a 400, a self pair is a 400, a missing or deleted vendor
risk is a 404, and an existing pair (in either order) is a 409.

The map draws a pair as a dashed related edge between the two vendor risk
nodes. Filtering the map to a vendor keeps vendor risks related to its own,
but not their children. The exposure report and the inheritance rules ignore
pairs: they read `inherits_from` only.

### Vendor risk insights

`pages/Vendors/VendorRiskInsights/` is the content of the **Insights** tab of the
Vendors page (`/vendors/insights`). It has a sub-tab per report: Heat map, Blast
radius, Duplicate vendor risks and Framework coverage. The report a viewer left
it on is kept in `localStorage` (`vendor-risk-insights`, stored as `{ "tab": … }`;
an older stored `open` is ignored). Only the active report is mounted, so each
report is fetched once its sub-tab is first shown. The heat map is drawn from
**every active vendor risk** (`useVendorRisks({ projectId: "all", vendorId: "all",
filter: "active" })` in `pages/Vendors/index.tsx`, the very same cached read as the
register while it is on its defaults), not from what the register is narrowed to:
the use case and **Risk status** controls are on the Risks tab, and the reports beside
the heat map read every vendor risk too. While those risks load, a skeleton stands in
place of the heat map only (`loading` of `VendorRiskInsights`); the other reports and
the sub-tab bar stay.

Choosing a heat map cell takes the viewer to the Risks tab (`/vendors/risks`). Before it
navigates, the page (`handleHeatCell`) resets the register view, so that the register
lists exactly the risks of the cell: the use case (back to all), **Risk status** (back to
Active), the filter conditions, the search and the risk level card are all
cleared (a summary card is not a filter condition, so `handleHeatCell` clears it itself), and
the toolbar shows that: the Risks tab mounts `TableFilter` afresh each time it opens, and the
page hands it its own state (`defaultConditions` and `defaultLogic` from `useFilterBy`), which
the reset has just emptied, so it opens blank. The same hand-over keeps a filter chosen on the
Risks tab when the viewer leaves the tab and comes back. There
`HeatCellNotice` (`VendorRiskInsights/HeatCellNotice.tsx`, `role="status"`) says "The
table shows only the risks in the selected heat map cell." with a **Show all risks**
link that clears the cell and leaves the viewer on the Risks tab, with the keyboard focus moved to the register (a `region` labelled "Vendor risks list", `tabIndex={-1}`), since the link is removed with the notice and the focus would otherwise fall to the page. The notice takes the
keyboard focus (`tabIndex={-1}`, `focusOnMount` and `onFocused`) when it opens because a
cell was chosen, since the heat map button the viewer used is gone with the tab; it does
not take the focus when the Risks tab is opened by hand with a cell still chosen. The cell
stays selected on the heat map while the filter lasts, and choosing the same cell again
on the heat map clears it without leaving the Insights tab.

| Section | Source | What it shows |
|---------|--------|---------------|
| Heat map | every active vendor risk (`useVendorRisks`, see above) | Likelihood × severity grid (`RiskHeatMap` in cell-select mode). Selecting a cell resets the register view, filters the register by the cell and opens the Risks tab; selecting the same cell again (from the heat map) clears the filter. |
| Blast radius | `GET /api/riskLinks/vendor-exposure` | Per vendor: vendor risks with children, distinct inheriting project risks, affected use cases, open suggestions. Admins get a "View on map" link to `/risk-inheritance?vendor=ID`. |
| Duplicate vendor risks | `GET /api/riskLinks/vendor-duplicates` | Pairs of one vendor's risks that read alike (description + impact, F7's tokeniser and threshold). Never compares two vendors' risks. |
| Framework coverage | `GET /api/riskLinks/vendor-coverage` | Mapped / gap / no framework, F8's three-state rule: a gap is an unmapped risk whose vendor serves a use case with a framework attached. |

Exposure counts **confirmed** children only; suggestions are shown beside it.
A child that inherits through two of one vendor's risks counts once for that
vendor. The same report feeds the table's **Inherited by** column ("3 project
risks", or "2 suggested" when nothing is confirmed yet). Its link opens the
vendor risk on its Linked risks tab, and its tooltip names the use cases.
Saving, deleting or restoring a vendor risk invalidates all three reports
(`VENDOR_INSIGHTS_KEY`) in the shared mutation hooks
(`hooks/useVendorRiskMutations.ts`), so a change made from a vendor's risk
dialog refreshes them too; so do a vendor update or delete. The page also
invalidates every read of the register (`vendorRiskQueryKeys.all`, in its
`refreshVendorRisks`), so that both the register and the heat map are read
again. These hooks and the vendor ones turn off the global error toast
(`suppressGlobalErrorToast`) because the modals and the page show their own,
one per failure; a delete that answers 404 says "Vendor not found." / "Risk not
found.". The **Inherited by** column belongs to the register on
the Risks tab, which reads the exposure report only while that tab is shown
(`useVendorExposure(enabled)`); the Blast radius report reads it itself.

### Candidate notices

The vendor counterpart of the model risk notices
(`services/riskLinks/vendorCandidates.ts`). The owner of a project risk hears
once, as a `vendor_risk_candidates` in-app notification, that a vendor serving
the risk's use case has vendor risks it could inherit from. It fires,
fire-and-forget, when a vendor is created with use cases, when a vendor update
adds use cases (only the added ones), and when a vendor risk is created on a
vendor that already has use cases (that vendor risk only). Risks already linked
to the vendor risk (any status), risks that already have a confirmed parent or
confirmed children, and soft-deleted risks are left out. At most 25 risks are
notified per trigger. Dedup is per (risk, vendor) for use-case triggers and per
vendor risk id for create triggers, backed by the
`notifications_vendor_risk_candidates_uniq` partial unique index. No link is
written; the owner decides from the Linked risks tab.

## Vendor Risk Suggestions

`GET /vendors/:id/riskSuggestions` is a **read-only report** (any signed-in member; 400 for an
id that isn't a positive integer, 404 when the organization has no such vendor). No screen
calls it yet. For one vendor it reads the four
questionnaire columns (`data_sensitivity`, `business_criticality`, `past_issues`,
`regulatory_exposure`), derives the vendor risks those answers imply, and returns them with the
field and raw value that produced each (`reasons`) and a derived `risk_level`. It **writes
nothing** — no `vendorrisks` row, no migration, no status column. The project link is a property
of the vendor (`vendors_projects`), not of a risk row, so nothing is bound to a project here.

A response reports `questionnaire_complete` (all four columns non-null), `existing_risk_count`,
the `suggestions`, and any `suppressed` archetypes. An unassessed vendor answers
`questionnaire_complete: false` with zero suggestions, which is deliberately distinct from a
fully answered "no exposure" vendor (`true`, zero suggestions). Suggestions are ordered worst
`risk_level` first, then by archetype.

### Archetypes

| # | Trigger | `risk_severity` | `likelihood` |
|---|---------|-----------------|--------------|
| A `data_sensitivity` | Any value except `None` and `Internal only`. `Health data (e.g. HIPAA)` → `Catastrophic`; `Financial data` / `Personally identifiable information (PII)` / `Model weights or AI assets` → `Major`; `Other sensitive data` → `Moderate` | per value | `Possible` |
| B `business_criticality` | `High (critical to core services or products)` | `Major` | `Unlikely` |
| C `past_issues` | `Minor incident (e.g. small delay, minor bug)` → `Minor`; `Major incident (e.g. data breach, legal issue)` → `Major` | per value | `Likely` |
| D `regulatory_exposure` | Any value except `None` | `Moderate` | `Possible` |

`risk_level` is derived with `calculateRiskLevel` from `utils/riskLevel.utils.ts`, the
formula a saved vendor risk gets, so accepting a suggestion stores the level it showed
(for example `Very high risk`). Suggestion text names the
answer through a short display label, not the raw enum value; `reasons` keeps the raw enum value.

### Suppression

Before returning, each archetype's `risk_description` is compared against the vendor's existing
active risks (`getVendorRisksByVendorIdQuery`, sliced to 200 in the service) using Jaccard
similarity over word tokens. Tokens are lowercased, stripped to `[a-z0-9 ]`, and any token of
length ≤ 2 is dropped. An archetype scoring **at or above
`VENDOR_SUGGESTION_SUPPRESS_THRESHOLD = 0.25`** is removed from `suggestions` and reported in
`suppressed` with the `matched_vendor_risk_id` — so a user can see which existing risk absorbed
it rather than watching it vanish.

## Vendor Research Agent

A background agent looks a vendor up on the open web and proposes risks for the vendor risk register. Its output is a list of **findings**: candidates, each with the quotes and links it rests on. **Nothing reaches the register until a person accepts a finding.**

The agent runs on the organization's own LLM key and needs nothing else to run; a Brave Search key is optional and makes the news of a key that has no search of its own more reliable. Its research is a loop in which the organization's model decides what to search for and which pages to read, using two actions that the server implements, `search` and `read`. The analysis that follows reads only the text those actions returned, and what it says is checked by code against that text before it is stored. The web access (the page guard, `robots.txt`, the search engine, the loop and its limits) is the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md). The search engine is picked from the research key and the organization's Brave key, in this order: Claude's own web search for an Anthropic key, else Brave Search when the organization stored a Brave key, else the news of the GDELT Project (see [Which key a run uses](#which-key-a-run-uses)). Without a Brave key there is still no search account and no search key; the release gate that the Bing feed had is closed.

Not covered: researching all vendors at once on demand (the [scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan) takes a few at a time), and findings in the user's language (the agent writes English).

### Flow

```
POST /vendors or /v1/vendors  (VENDOR_RESEARCH_AUTO_RUN on)   POST /v1/vendors/:id/research  (vendor.edit)   hourly scan job (an interval is chosen)
          └─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘
                                ▼
            startVendorResearch: checks, then a vendor_research_runs row (queued)
                                ▼
            BullMQ job `vendor_research` on the `vendor-research` queue
                                ▼
            worker: handleVendorResearchJob → runVendorResearch
                                ▼
            run completed (findings stored) or failed
                                ▼
            Vendors > Research findings: Preview suggested risk (the vendor risk form opens on the finding; saving it accepts the finding) or Dismiss
```

**Triggers**

- **Automatic.** `createVendor` calls `triggerAutoResearch` after the vendor is committed and does not wait for it, so it cannot fail the response. It starts nothing for a demo vendor or when `VENDOR_RESEARCH_AUTO_RUN` is `false`, and logs why. Otherwise it goes through `startVendorResearch`. Nothing returns early for a missing search setting, because there is none; when the start refuses for a missing LLM key or a reached daily cap, the reason is logged and no run is created. So in an organization with an LLM key every new vendor costs one run on that key, bounded by the daily cap (see [Limits](#limits)), and `VENDOR_RESEARCH_AUTO_RUN=false` turns the automatic part off.
- **Manual.** `POST /v1/vendors/:id/research`, from the **Research risks** button on the vendors table or **Research again** on the Research findings tab.
- **Scheduled.** The hourly job of the scan starts the research of the vendors that are due, in each organization whose Admin chose an interval in **Research settings**. See [The scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan).

All three go through `startVendorResearch` (`services/vendorResearch/trigger.ts`). Its checks, and so the 404, 409, 429 and 503 answers, are described under [Vendor Research Endpoints](#vendor-research-endpoints).

**The run** (`runVendorResearch` in `pipeline.ts`, called by the job in `job.ts`)

1. **Start.** The run moves from `queued` to `running`, and the same statement (`markRunRunningQuery`) writes it to the ledger of the daily cap (`vendor_research_run_ledger`, see [Limits](#limits)). Only a queued run starts, and only while it was created less than 15 minutes ago (`STALE_RUN_MINUTES`, the limit of a **queued** run: the start, the sweep of queued runs, the cooldown and the daily cap share it, see [Limits](#limits)). The job skips a run in any other state, and one that has waited longer, without a web request or a model call; such a queued run stays `queued` until the sweep fails it as `timed out`.
2. **Load.** The vendor is loaded and the models are resolved before the research (see [Which key a run uses](#which-key-a-run-uses)), so an organization without a key makes no web request. A missing vendor or key fails the run.
3. **Research.** The research loop (`researchAgent.ts`), see [Research](#research): at most 24 steps and 5 minutes. Every snippet and page excerpt it gets goes to a ledger, tagged with a category.
4. **Evidence pack.** Before it is built, the pages where the vendor states its terms are compared with the ones that earlier runs kept, and each page that changed adds a source (see [Policy page changes](#policy-page-changes)). The ledger is then cleaned, numbered `S1`, `S2`, ... and trimmed to the size limit. The pack is stored on the run. If the web answered but nothing usable came back, the run completes with no findings and no model call.
5. **Model call.** One structured call over the pack only, see [Model call](#model-call).
6. **Validation.** Deterministic checks of everything the model said, see [Validation](#validation).
7. **De-duplication and caps.** Candidates that repeat something known are dropped, then at most 3 per category and 8 per run are kept. See [De-duplication](#de-duplication).
8. **Checks of the findings that stay.** At the same time, the pages of the findings that rest on snippets are read and the verifier asks one model call about all of them. See [The check of a finding that rests on snippets](#the-check-of-a-finding-that-rests-on-snippets) and [The verifier](#the-verifier).
9. **Store.** One transaction inserts the findings and completes the run. If it fails, nothing is saved and the run ends `failed`. After it commits, the run records its sources and keeps the pages where the vendor states its terms (see [The memory of the sources](#the-memory-of-the-sources) and [Policy page changes](#policy-page-changes)); neither can change the run.
10. **Notice.** After an automatic run that stored at least one finding, the vendor's assignee is told (see [Notice to the assignee](#notice-to-the-assignee)). This comes after the run has ended and cannot change it.

A run ends once. It is `failed` when the vendor or the LLM key is missing, when the model call fails (in the loop when it found no usable source, or in the analysis), when the web never answered the loop and nothing is left to analyse, when the findings cannot be saved, when it runs past its time limit (`timed out`), or when something unexpected happens. The web never answered when every call of the loop failed and the ledger holds no usable source (`The run stopped because the web could not be reached`), or when the model never searched or read (`The model did not use the research actions`). Records of the public databases are sources without a call of the loop, so a run whose calls all failed but that found records analyses them and completes, with `search_failed` in the categories whose calls all failed; a run that found no record, or only records that an earlier run showed (a scheduled run), still fails. Otherwise the run is `completed`, also with no findings: a run whose calls were answered but returned nothing is a result, and so is partial research. A loop that stopped early, for its time, its steps or a model error with sources in hand, still hands what it found to the analysis. The pipeline catches model and web errors itself and ends the run as `failed`, so the retries of the job (see [Automations](../infrastructure/automations.md#on-demand-jobs)) only cover Redis and database failures that happen before the run is marked `running`, while it is still `queued`.

### Categories

| Category | A finding is raised when the sources show | Where the agent looks | Lookback |
|----------|-------------------------------------------|-----------------------|----------|
| `security_incidents` | A breach, security incident or exploited vulnerability involving the vendor | The news first, then the whole web | 24 months |
| `certifications` | A certification claim that is limited, in progress, expired, out of scope or contradicted | The vendor's own trust and security pages, and the web, with the FedRAMP Marketplace and the C2PA conforming products list of the vendor risk database as context | 24 months |
| `legal_regulatory` | A fine, enforcement action, sanction, lawsuit or settlement | The news first, then the whole web, and the sanctions lists, the Consolidated Screening List and the enforcement, case and product safety lists of the vendor risk database | 36 months |
| `ai_specific` | Customer data used for training, AI subprocessors or model providers that are not disclosed, or other adverse AI terms; also an entity with the vendor's name in a list about AI (a case study of MITRE ATLAS, NHTSA's crash reports, the FDA list of AI-enabled devices, a recall of such a device, a court decision in the AI Hallucination Cases database, an incident of the PipeRoll registry), matched by name only | The vendor's own privacy policy, terms, data processing agreement and subprocessor list, the web, and the AI lists of the vendor risk database | 24 months |
| `availability` | An outage or a lasting degradation of the vendor's service that affected its customers, or a pattern of them | The news first, then the whole web, and the incident history on the vendor's status page | 12 months |
| `financial_health` | Layoffs, a funding shortfall, insolvency or bankruptcy, or a sale or a change of control of the vendor, that threatens the continuity or the quality of its service (a funding round, growth or hiring is not a risk) | The news first, then the whole web, and, for a vendor with a stored LEI, its GLEIF record as context | 12 months |

The lookback is counted back from the day of the run and is about the date of a source, not the date of the events it describes. The search leaves out a result with a known date before the window, and the analysis is told the windows too (a source inside the window still counts when it reports an older matter that is still open). A source without a date is kept, and a page on the vendor's own domain is exempt. The windows are in `LOOKBACK_MONTHS` (`categories.ts`). A vendor without a domain can be researched in the four categories that search the news; `certifications` and `ai_specific` read the vendor's website.

A missing mention is never a finding: "no SOC 2 page found" is recorded as `nothing_found` in the run summary, not as a risk.

The vendor's **domain** is the identity anchor. It comes from the `website` field (scheme, `www.`, path and port are removed; an IP address or `localhost` is not a domain). With one, the agent may read the vendor's own pages, and those of its subdomains, without a search result for them first. Without one, the loop offers only `security_incidents` and `legal_regulatory`, `read` follows only URLs that a search returned, and `certifications` and `ai_specific` are `skipped` with the note "Vendor website is missing or not a valid domain". Nothing prefers a domain in a search engine: the search is general, and the vendor's site is reached with `read`.

### Research

The loop (`services/webResearch/researchLoop.ts`, set up for a vendor by `researchAgent.ts` with the prompts of `prompts.ts`) asks the model for one step per turn, as a JSON object with a thought and up to four actions, and runs the actions in code (the reads that follow one another run together, at most two at once from one host). It uses the same self-correcting structured call as the analysis and not native tool calling, so it works with every model that can answer the app's other structured calls (OpenAI, Anthropic, DeepSeek, custom endpoints, local models). A model that cannot answer in JSON fails the run with a clear message. Each turn the model sees the vendor (name, domain, what it provides), today's date, the categories it may research with their windows, a briefing of what the code found out before the first step (see the check of the website below), the history of its steps and the budget that is left. Everything that came from the web or from the model goes into a prompt escaped.

| Action | Needs | What the code does |
|--------|-------|--------------------|
| `search` | `query`, `category`, and optionally `kind` (`web` or `news`) | Asks the search engine of the run, behind the `SearchEngine` interface: Claude's own web search for an Anthropic key, Brave Search for every other key when the organization has a Brave key, and the news of the GDELT Project otherwise. The kinds depend on the engine: `web` and `news` for Claude's search and Brave, `news` only for GDELT; with no `kind` the search is a web search when the engine answers the web and a news search when it does not, and a search of a kind the engine does not answer is refused for free with a fixed phrase (no budget, no call recorded). Up to 10 results, each with title, address, date when there is one (a result of Claude's search has none; GDELT dates an article by when it saw it, within the last 3 months; Brave gives the page's age or the article's date as a day) and snippet (GDELT gives none, only the title; Brave gives passages of the page or the article's description). A result older than the window of its category, one that does not name the vendor in its title, snippet or address, and one that repeats an earlier result is left out and counted, and the model is told how many |
| `read` | `url`, `category`, and optionally `focus` (a few keywords) or `part` (the number of a part of a long page that was read before) | Allowed only for an address that a search of this run returned or that is on the vendor's domain (not when that domain is exactly a host where many have pages, such as `github.com`: see the ledger below). Then the URL and address policy, `robots.txt`, a guarded fetch and the text of the page (a PDF, up to 10 MiB, is read in a worker thread with limits on time, memory and pages; a scan with no text layer cannot be read; a page that a script builds is read from the data it carries, and with `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS` on, in a headless browser as the last resort, see [Rendering a page that a script builds](../infrastructure/web-research.md#rendering-a-page-that-a-script-builds)). The model gets an excerpt of at most 8,000 characters, and for a page of the vendor up to 25 links to its relevant pages. A page that is longer is also cut into parts of at most 8,000 characters (40 at most), the result says how many, and a `read` of the same address with `part` shows another one: it comes from the page the run holds, so it is no request and no unit of the budget of reads. The first two parts that the model asks for are kept as sources |
| `finish` | nothing | Ends the research. If the ledger is empty, or a category that can be researched has no call, the first `finish` is answered once with a reminder of what is missing, and the second is final |

The limits: 24 steps (a step is a model turn and holds up to four actions; invalid ones are included), 8 `search` actions, 12 `read` actions (a read that failed gives its unit back, at most six times a run, and a page that failed for good is not tried again), 60 seconds for one model turn and 5 minutes for the loop. The full table, and the page guard behind `read`, are in the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-research-loop). The `robots.txt` files, the pages and the searches are also kept in Redis for a while and shared by all runs (24 hours, 15 minutes and 10 minutes), so a second run of the same vendor costs fewer requests; see [the shared cache](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-shared-cache). A model that answers badly cannot crash the loop or keep it going: an action that breaks its rule is answered with the reason (the other actions of its step still run), and an action that the rules refuse never reaches the web. A blocked page, a failed search and an empty answer are results, never exceptions that fail a run. When a budget is spent the model is told and must finish; when the steps or the time run out, the loop ends and the analysis runs on the ledger as it is.

**The check of the website.** For a vendor with a domain, the researcher reads the home page once before the first step (`preflight.ts`, through the run's page reader, outside the budget and the ledger) and gives the model a briefing: whether the site could be read, whether the home page names the vendor (by its name, never by the domain), where it went when it redirected off the vendor's site, and up to 12 of its links to security, trust, privacy and legal pages, which the model can read without reading the home page first. A site that does not answer (the bare domain and the `www.` form are both tried), that the `robots.txt` or the site itself refuses, or that the address policy refuses, is **closed** for the run: its pages are refused as `site_closed` without a request or a unit of the budget, and the two categories that need the website, if the model made no call for them, are recorded as pages that could not be read, so their outcome reads `search_failed` with "The pages could not be read". A home page that does not name the vendor is reported to the model as a warning (the domain may be another company's) but changes nothing in code. The check is the first step of the run trace. See the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-research-loop).

**The routine work before the first step.** After the check, and before the first model turn, code does what every run starts with (`gather.ts`, switched on by `gather: true`, which the job sets): the news searches for the news categories (`<name> data breach`, `security incident`, `lawsuit`, `fined`, `outage`, `layoffs`, `bankruptcy`, `acquisition`, the authority of the vendor's country and `settlement` for the legal news (`<name> FTC`), and one more for each category that a cheap model call, asked while the website is checked, gives from what the model remembers about the vendor: a query only, never evidence, `hints.ts`), the reads of the best two results of each news category (the title names the vendor, then the credibility of the source, then the date; different publishers first) and the reads of the vendor's own pages for `certifications` and `ai_specific` (the pages its home page links to and its `sitemap.xml` lists, at most three per category; the pages that sites usually have, such as `/security`, `/trust` and `/privacy`, only for a category left with fewer than two). The site is not read when it is closed, when the home page redirected off the vendor's website, or when the home page does not name the vendor. The model is told what was collected and spends its steps on what is missing. This work is paid for apart (it uses none of the model's 8 searches and 12 reads, and a read that failed by code gives nothing back), goes through the same session and rules as the model's own calls, and is traced as steps with `by: "code"`. See the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-research-loop).

**One loop for each category.** After the routine work, the model researches each category the vendor allows in a loop of its own, all at the same time (`perCategory: true`, which the job sets): a loop has at most four model turns, a share of the model's 8 searches and 12 reads (a category of the vendor's website one search, the four news categories share the six that remain, 2, 2, 1 and 1 in the order of the categories, and the 12 reads are shared out evenly, 2 for each of the six categories; a vendor without a domain has four loops with 2 searches and 3 reads each), a task that names its category and a briefing of what the code collected for that category only. A loop for the news that the code already searched and found nothing for finishes at its first turn. The run ends as its loops do (time, steps, budget, finished; a model error only when every loop failed), and the trace names the loop of every step and model call. See the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-research-loop).

**The ledger.** One entry per URL: `{ category, kind, onVendorSite, url, title, content, publishedAt }`. A search result is an entry of kind `result` with its snippet. Reading a page replaces the snippet of the same URL with the excerpt (kind `page`, same title), and the date is the page's own when it has one, else the snippet's (a result of Claude's search has no snippet and no date; a GDELT result has a title and the day GDELT saw the article). A result of Brave Search is the exception: its entry carries `transient: true` (set from `SearchResult.transient`, which Brave puts on each result), and a page read in its place keeps nothing of it: the title is the page's own (the host of the address when the page has none) and the date the page's own or none, and the mark is dropped (see [Brave text is never stored](#brave-text-is-never-stored)). `onVendorSite` records whether the content came from the vendor's own domain, decided on the URL that answered: a page that redirected off the domain is not the vendor's own, and one asked for elsewhere that answered from the vendor's site is. A domain that is exactly a host where many have pages (`isSharedPlatformHost` in `sharedHosts.ts`: the vendor whose website is `github.com/acme` has the domain `github.com`) is no own domain, as the vendor's domain or as one it moved to: no page of the host is the vendor's own, can be read without a search result or skips the lookback. A site under such a host (`acme.github.io`) is the vendor's alone and stays its own. The prompt (`site="vendor"`) and the [validation](#validation) take the vendor's own page from this flag alone, never from the host of the URL that was asked for. A second read of a URL changes nothing, but a `read` with `part` adds the part to the content of the entry behind a gap marker (`[…]` on a line of its own), for the first two parts a page gives. The key is the **canonical address** (tracking parameters and amp wrappers removed, a quotation mark at the end refused, the address a page claims for itself taken when it is the same document), and a page whose text repeats a page already read for the category, or a search result that repeats the title and snippet of an earlier one, is not a second entry (see the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-research-loop)). Every call is also recorded with its category and whether the web answered it, which feeds the [category outcomes](#category-outcomes).

**Evidence of two kinds.** `buildEvidencePack` (`evidence.ts`) turns the ledger into the pack the analysis reads. Control characters and lone surrogates are cleaned, an unusable URL is dropped, and the first entry of a URL wins across categories. The sources are `kind="result"` (a snippet, at most 1,500 characters) or `kind="page"` (an excerpt of at most 8,000 characters, and up to two more parts behind a gap marker each, 24,010 characters at most), in the order of the categories and then of the loop. Until the pack is at most 100,000 bytes, a record of a list whose records never raise a risk (`CONTEXT_ONLY_RECORD_SOURCES`: the registers and status lists, see [Credibility](#credibility)) is removed first, the last one of the category that holds the most bytes among those that have one and two or more sources; when there is none, the last source of the category that holds the most bytes among those with two or more sources is removed (in both steps a tie goes to the later category). A category with one source keeps it, and when every category is down to one the content of the largest source is cut instead, so a category that was researched always has a source in the pack and cannot read as "nothing found" for want of room. The ids `S1`, `S2`, ... are numbered after the trimming, without gaps. The pack is stored on the run.

**Prompt version.** `CREDIBILITY_PROMPT_VERSION` (`prompts.ts`) is `vr-20`: the one version of the prompts of a run, since there is one set of rules. It is stored on every run and changes whenever the wording of a prompt changes. It moves once per release of prompt changes, not once per edit. `vr-2` to `vr-6` were the versions of the confidence rules: the web agent's two prompts, the rule of a query (the vendor's name and at most three topic words), the anchors of the likelihood and the severity and the one-sentence rationale of a finding, a loop step of up to four actions with the routine work in its briefing and the prompts of the hints and the verifier (see [The verifier](#the-verifier)), the reading of a long page in parts, and the sources that begin with `Page change` (see [Policy page changes](#policy-page-changes)). `vr-7` to `vr-10` belong to the vendor risk database (`vr-7` was never stored): the analysis no longer asks for a confidence, the sanctions lists are worded (the UK and EU lists beside OFAC's; an entry matched by name only is written "An entity named … is on …", and one matched by the vendor's verified website or one of its identifiers is about the vendor unless the identifiers it lists say otherwise) and the CVE Program and the UN list are named. `vr-11` puts the search engine into the loop prompt: Bing is gone, so the prompt describes only the kinds of search that the engine of the run answers, makes the news the default kind when the web is not one of them, and says how far back the news reaches. `vr-12` tells the verifier which public database a cited record is an entry of. `vr-13` adds the AI databases: the analysis prompt names the AI Incident Database, MITRE ATLAS, the NHTSA crash reports and the FDA list beside the sanctions lists, asks the same caution of an entry matched by name only (which every entry of them is: at most one risk, in AI-specific risks, likelihood Possible at most, an action plan that checks who the vendor is) and says that a list of authorized devices is no risk by itself, and the verifier's database names hold the four marks. It was not run against the golden set. `vr-14` (2026-11-07) adds two lists to those wordings. The sanctions paragraph names the US Consolidated Screening List: its List line names the list, and a listing on the Entity List or the Military End User List means that an export licence is required, not that the entity is sanctioned; the rules of the sanctions paragraph then apply to it word for word. The AI paragraph names the FDA recalls beside the AI databases that raise at most one risk each, written "appears in": a recall of a device on the FDA's list of AI-enabled devices is the firm's correction or removal of a device, the recalling firm may be a subsidiary or a distributor of the maker, and a recall is not a finding that the AI failed. The verifier's database names hold the two new marks, `US Consolidated Screening List` and `FDA medical device recalls (openFDA)`. `vr-14` was not run against the golden set either, and its new wording reaches only a source whose text begins with one of the two new marks. `vr-15` (2026-11-10) words the lists of the third round by their class (see **The third round of lists** above). Canada's sanctions list joins the sanctions paragraph, and new paragraphs word the AI incident lists, the enforcement and case lists, the product safety lists, the data breach notice list and the register or status lists. An entry of a list that can raise a risk raises at most one, written as an entry of the list ("appears in", or "reported a data breach to the California Attorney General") and never as a finding against the vendor: a case, an investigation, a recall or a notice is not a finding or a ruling, and an OFSI notice is not a designation. An entry of a register or status list raises none, whatever it says, and is never evidence that the vendor or its product is safe, compliant or approved. The verifier's database names hold the 23 new marks (39 in all). Like `vr-13` and `vr-14` it was not run against the golden set, and its new wording reaches only a source whose text begins with one of the new marks. `vr-16` (2026-11-11) stops telling a record or a change from a web page by the words its text begins with, since a page that third parties wrote can begin with `SEC EDGAR`, `Page change` or the name of a sanctions list. Each source of the analysis carries the attributes that only code writes from its provenance (`provenanceOf` in `evidence.ts`, passed to `buildUserPrompt`): `database="…"` for a record, with the name the verifier is told (`VERIFIER_DATABASE_NAMES`), and `change="terms"` for a change of a page of the vendor's terms (see [Policy page changes](#policy-page-changes)). The rules of the databases, the lists, the status page and a change key on those attributes, and a source without them is an ordinary search result or page whatever its text begins with. The text of a genuine record is unchanged. It was not run against the golden set either. `vr-17` (2026-11-12) stops saying that every record of SEC EDGAR is the vendor's own filing. A CIK found by the vendor's name (Q3) may be another company's, and its record now carries the lookup note of a match by name only. The SEC sentence says a filing is the vendor's own only without that note, and with it the filing is written as the filing of a company of that name. It was not run against the golden set. `vr-18` (2026-11-12) says what a line of a change of the vendor's terms that begins with the continued mark (`…`) is: a long clause of the page is kept in parts, and every part after the first begins with that mark, so such a line continues the line before it on the page and is not a new clause. It was not run against the golden set; it matters only for a change of a clause longer than 600 characters. `vr-19` (2026-11-12) follows the way a change now shows such a clause (see [Policy page changes](#policy-page-changes)): a line that begins with two spaces and no dash continues the line before it on the page, the two are one clause with one space between them, and a quote may run across them; a line that begins with `…` is a later part of a long clause, quoted on its own and never with the line before it (a part keeps that mark when its part before is not shown, or was cut where no blank was). It was not run against the golden set either. `vr-20` (2026-11-23) writes the example action of the loop prompt in the order of the step schema (`action`, `category`, `query`, `kind`, `url`, `focus`, `part`: `LOOP_ACTION_FIELDS` of `researchLoop.ts`) and asks for the fields of an action in that order. A provider that decodes against the schema (Claude through an OpenAI-compatible gateway, for one) accepts the fields in the schema's order only: the old example wrote `kind` before `query`, a model that copied it could no longer write the query, and every search of a run was refused. The prompts of the confidence rules (`vr-6`) were removed with the flag; a run from before them keeps its `vr-6` in `prompt_version`.

### Which key a run uses

An organization can have several LLM keys, and the two jobs of a run suit different models: the research loop makes many small calls (a fast, inexpensive model is enough), and the analysis makes one large call that rates the risks (a stronger model can help). An Admin chooses one key for each in **Research settings** (see [Frontend](#vendor-research)), or leaves either to the default.

`resolveOrgModel` (`pipeline.ts`) reads the organization's keys, newest first, and the choice (`getVendorResearchSettingsQuery`), and `selectKeys` (`keySelection.ts`) picks:

| Choice | Research uses | Analysis uses |
|--------|---------------|---------------|
| Nothing chosen | The newest key | The key of the research |
| Only a research key | That key | The key of the research |
| Only an analysis key | The newest key | That key |
| Both | The research key | The analysis key |

A chosen key that is not in the organization's list (deleted, so that a stale read still names it) counts as not chosen, and an analysis key that is the research key means one model does both. **A setting never fails a run**: if the choice cannot be read, the run goes on with the defaults and the log says only that (the error's name). An organization with no key still gets no run (`No LLM key configured`), and the keys are never logged.

**The search of a run follows its research key and the organization's Brave key.** The key that does the research decides which search engine the run uses (`searchEngineOf`, which reads the provider and the address of the key and never the key; see [Search engines](../infrastructure/web-research.md#search-engines-q21-decided-2026-10-31)), and a key with no search of its own searches with Brave when the organization stored a Brave Search key (`searchEngineWith`). The order is Claude's search, then the organization's Brave key, then GDELT:

| Research key | Search | Fee |
|--------------|--------|-----|
| Anthropic, with the Anthropic API address or none | Claude's own web search (the `web_search_20250305` tool), the web and the news | About $10 per 1,000 searches, billed by Anthropic to the key, plus the tokens of the search calls. A run makes about 20 to 30 searches |
| Any other key (OpenAI, OpenRouter, a custom endpoint such as DeepSeek, a local model, Bedrock, or an Anthropic key behind a gateway), when the organization has a Brave key | Brave Search, the web (its LLM Context endpoint, or web search on a plan without it) and the news (its news endpoint), with no stated limit of reach | Brave's own price, billed by Brave to the organization's Brave account: about $5 per 1,000 searches after $5 of free credit a month (Brave asks for a card). A run makes about 20 to 30 searches, like a run on Claude's search |
| Any other key, when the organization has no Brave key | The news of the last 3 months through the GDELT Project, one request for the run | None |

`resolveOrgModel` (`pipeline.ts`) reads the Brave key with the other keys (`getBraveKeyQuery`); a key that cannot be read does not fail the run, which searches as an organization without one (the log says so, with the error's name only). `searchSpecOf` puts the Brave key on the run's `SearchSpec` as a property that is not enumerable, so a log line, a JSON copy or a trace of the spec never holds it. `createRunSearch` (`researchAgent.ts`) builds the engine: a `brave` run gets `withFallback(brave, gdelt)`, and an `anthropic` run gets `withFallback(cached Claude, brave ? withFallback(brave, gdelt) : gdelt)`, so Brave stands between Claude's search and GDELT when the organization has a key. An Anthropic key whose model cannot run Claude's search searches with Brave as well when there is a key. A Brave key that Brave refuses, a 402, or a quota that is used up (a 429 whose `X-RateLimit-Remaining` shows 0 past the per-second window, or a second 429 after the one wait) is a `SearchUnavailableError` and moves the rest of the run to GDELT. Brave is never put behind the search cache (`withSearchCache`).

An Anthropic key whose provider refuses to search (web search turned off for the organization, or a model or tool the key cannot use) falls back to Brave when the organization has a Brave key, and to GDELT otherwise, for the rest of the run. A run stores the engine whose searches were answered in `search_engine`. `withFallback` asks a nested fallback which engine answered (its `usedId`), so a run is credited to `brave` after Claude was refused and Brave answered, to `gdelt` after Brave was refused too and GDELT answered, and still to `brave` when Brave answered and was refused later with GDELT answering nothing. The run summary says "Searched with Claude web search", "Searched with Brave Search" or "News searched with the GDELT Project (last 3 months)", the last with a link to the project, as the GDELT terms ask. The research settings tell an Admin the fees. GDELT asks for one request every 5 seconds from an address, and it sometimes answers 429 on its own (on 2026-11-01 it refused 11 of 12 requests from one machine). After a 429 the process leaves GDELT alone for 60 seconds. A run does not lose its news for that: its one ask waits out the pause and asks again, every search that needs the news waits for that same ask (a search leaves at once when its own time is up), and the clock of the code's routine work moves by each wait plus one ask, up to 170 seconds in all (see [the routine work](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-research-loop)). Once GDELT has refused the asks of a run `GDELT_MAX_ASKS` = 3 times, that is the answer of the run and its searches fail at once. Only a 429 is waited out and asked again this way and only a 429 counts towards the cap (a timeout, a 5xx after its one `Retry-After` wait, a body that is not JSON and a `robots.txt` that disallows are not). Each 429 is counted (`vendor_research_gdelt_refusals_total`) and written to the run's trace as `gdelt_refused` (`gdelt_refused_final` for the third), so a person who opens the run can see why it lost its news. A failure that is not a 429 writes one step for the run with a fixed word of its kind (`gdelt_not_json` for a 200 that is not JSON, which is how GDELT refuses a query, `gdelt_unreachable`, `gdelt_server_error`, `gdelt_robots`, `gdelt_bad_status`, `gdelt_bad_answer`, `gdelt_no_query` for a vendor with no name that can be asked for, and `gdelt_failed`), see [web research](../infrastructure/web-research.md) (*The news of GDELT*). A run whose news searches all fail reads "The search was not available" in the categories that rest on the news (see [Category outcomes](#category-outcomes)). An analysis key makes no search. The search engine replaces the Bing feed that the first versions read (removed on 2026-10-31).

#### Brave text is never stored

Brave's API terms (§3(b)(i), read 2026-11-23) allow the output of a call (titles, snippets, the passages of LLM Context) to be used during the run, not kept. The model sees Brave's text in full; the run stores none of it. Every Brave result carries `transient: true` (`SearchResult.transient`, set per result rather than per engine, so that an answer in flight when a fallback switches engines keeps its mark), and each place a run stores text enforces the rule:

- **The ledger.** The web session marks the entry of such a result `transient`. A page read in its place takes the page's own title (the host of its address when the page has none) and the page's own date or none, never the search's (`webSession.ts`).
- **The evidence.** `progress.evidence`, the `evidence` column, is written through `storedEvidenceOf` (`evidence.ts`): a transient result keeps `id`, `category`, `kind`, `onVendorSite` and `url`, with an empty `title` and `content` and a `null` `publishedAt`. Pages and every other source are kept as they are.
- **The sources of a finding.** `analyse` and `checkSnippetFindings` take the addresses of the transient results (`transientUrlsOf`). A source among them keeps its quote only when a page read during the [check of a finding that rests on snippets](#the-check-of-a-finding-that-rests-on-snippets), on the cited site, holds the quote: the quote is then the publisher's own text, with the page's title (or its host) and the page's date or none. Otherwise the source is stored by address only: `title: ""`, `quote: ""`, `published_at: null` and `quote_withheld: true` (`withholdSearchText` in `verifySnippets.ts`). This holds for every finding that cites such a source, a finding that also cites a page and a run with no page reader included. Every writer of sources (insert, the credibility label, the relabel and the backfill) spreads the source, so the scrubbed values stay; accepting a finding copies no sources.
- **The trace.** When any ledger entry is transient, or the researcher reports that a search answered with a transient result (`ResearchResult.transientText`), the pipeline calls `trace.withholdRejectionText()`: the snapshot stores every rejection with an empty `text` and every step with an empty `thought`, since a candidate or a thought the model wrote can copy the search text word for word. A run whose search spec carries a Brave key makes that call before the research starts as well, so a run that times out or whose research fails, and saves its trace before the research reports, stores no thought either.
- **No cache.** The Brave engine is never wrapped in `withSearchCache`, so no Brave answer reaches Redis.
- **No live evaluation.** `npm run eval:vendor-research -- --live` never searches with Brave (`withoutBrave` in `searchEngineOf.ts`), since it writes what the web answered (`--record`) and the trace (`--trace`) to files: a `brave` spec searches GDELT there, and an `anthropic` one loses its Brave fallback.

A stored finding whose source is withheld shows that source as a link to its host with no quote and the caption "Based on a search excerpt; the source page could not be read." (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)).

**The key check.** `PUT /v1/vendor-research/search-key` checks a key before it stores it (`checkBraveKey` in `services/vendorResearch/braveKeyCheck.ts`). The text must be 10 to 200 printable ASCII characters with no whitespace or control character (a loop over the characters, not a regular expression), else it is `invalid_key` with no request. Then the two endpoints a run asks are checked at the same time, LLM Context (`/llm/context`) and news (`/news/search`), each with the address `braveUrlOf("test", kind, 1)` builds (one result; when LLM Context answers `400 OPTION_NOT_IN_PLAN`, web search is checked in its place, since a run will ask it), through `safeFetch` with the key in `X-Subscription-Token`, an 8 second limit and at most 1 MB of each answer read. An endpoint answering `error.code` `SUBSCRIPTION_TOKEN_INVALID`, a 401, a 402 or a 403 refuses the key, and either refusal makes it `invalid_key`; a 2xx or a 429 (a valid key over its rate) is a pass, and the key is valid only when both endpoints pass; otherwise (a network error, a timeout or any other status) it is `unreachable`. Only the status or the error's name is logged, never the key or the answer.

What the choice changes: the run's `model` column holds `<research model> / <analysis model>` when the two differ, and the id of the one model otherwise; each model call of the [run trace](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-run-trace) records the model that made it; and the fallback to plain JSON that the loop may learn for a provider that does not support structured output (see the toolkit) is **not** carried over to an analysis on a different model, because it was learned about the other one.

### The scheduled scan

An Admin can have the research done again on a schedule. In **Research settings** the **Scheduled scan** select takes **Off** (the default), **Every week**, **Every 2 weeks** and **Every month**, which are 7, 14 and 30 days in `vendor_research_settings.scan_interval_days`. An organization that never chooses sees no change.

A repeating job of the `vendor-research` queue, `vendor_research_scan`, runs at 11 minutes past every hour (UTC). `jobs/producer.ts` registers it when the backend starts (`scheduleVendorResearchScan`, a BullMQ job scheduler named like the job: registering it again replaces its options, or changes nothing when they are the same, and it does not empty the queue, which holds the runs that are waiting). The worker of the queue tells it from the research of a run by its name and calls `runVendorResearchScan` (`scan.ts`). For each organization that chose an interval the scan:

1. counts the runs of the last 24 hours, whoever started them, as the daily cap counts them (`countRunsSinceQuery`, the started runs of a deleted vendor included), and works only while that is under **half of the daily cap** (25 with the default of 50), so that a person can always start a run by hand;
2. asks for the vendors that are **due** (`listDueVendorsQuery`): not a demo vendor, and without a run that counts toward the limits (see [Limits](#limits)) created within the interval, whoever started that run, so a run by hand or the automatic one after the vendor was created puts the vendor back to the end of the line. A run that failed after it started counts toward the cooldown and the daily cap, but holds its vendor back from the scan for one day only (`FAILED_RUN_BACKOFF_DAYS` in `utils/vendorResearch.utils.ts`), not for the whole interval: a run that failed for a passing reason (a rate limit, an outage, a restarted worker) is tried again the next day. A vendor whose runs keep failing therefore costs one run a day at most, inside the half of the cap that the scan may use. The order still goes by the newest run that counts, failed or not, so such a vendor does not take the first places of every batch. The vendors researched longest ago, or never, come first, and no more are asked for than the room under half of the cap or **5** (`SCAN_BATCH_SIZE`), so that a large register is spread over the days and not started in one hour;
3. starts a run for each through `startVendorResearch` with the trigger `scheduled` and no requesting user, so each run meets the checks of a run that a person starts (the vendor, an LLM key, an active run, the daily cap; the cooldown is for manual starts only). A start that answers `llm_key_missing`, `daily_cap` or `enqueue_failed` ends the organization for this scan, because the next vendor would meet the same answer.
