- **The script.** The default is a dry run, which writes nothing. `--apply` writes, with no flag to turn on: every run is rated by the credibility rules, and the reads already show the old confidence as a legacy label. `--undo` clears what the backfill wrote (a rule `legacy_confidence` or one that starts with `backfill_`), but for the `backfill_` label of a finding a person accepted or dismissed since: that label is what they decided the finding by (D4.6), and a later `--apply` would give the finding its old confidence instead, so it is kept and counted ("organization 1: cleared 3, kept 1 (decided after the backfill)"). `--organization N` limits the work to one organization (exit 2 for one that does not exist). It walks the organizations in id order, one at a time, and reads the findings without a label of one organization in batches of 500, each after the last id; every statement but the list of the organizations filters on `organization_id`. The write is guarded by `status = :status AND credibility IS NULL`, so a finding a person decided, or a live run labelled, since the read is skipped and counted; a later run of the script gives one decided meanwhile its carried-over label. It prints organization ids and counts only ("organization 1: 3 pending (high 1, medium 0, low 2), 2 decided (legacy 2)", "total: 3 pending, 2 decided; dry run, nothing written", "written 4, skipped 1 (decided or labelled meanwhile)"), and an error by its name. Exit codes: 0 done, 1 the database failed, 2 usage or refusal.

Run it where the servers run. The backend image has no `npx` (`Servers/Dockerfile` removes npm and npx), so a deployment from the image runs the compiled script, `dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js`, in the backend's container (`docker compose exec backend …`, or `kubectl exec -n <namespace> deploy/backend -- …` with the Helm chart, whose deployment is named `backend`). From a checkout, `npx ts-node` runs the source.

```bash
docker compose exec backend node dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js            # dry run
docker compose exec backend node dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js --apply
docker compose exec backend node dist/scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.js --undo
cd Servers && npx ts-node scripts/vendorResearchBackfillCredibility.ts                         # from a checkout
```

### Limits

| Limit | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Steps of the research loop | 24, invalid ones included |
| `search` actions per run | 8, at most 2 of a run at once (`MAX_SEARCHES_AT_ONCE` in `webSession.ts`); Claude's web search: at most 4 calls at once for the engine of a run and 200 output tokens each (`providerSearch.ts`); GDELT: a body of at most 1,048,576 bytes and 20,000 ms for a request, at least 5,000 ms between two requests of a process (`gdeltSearch.ts`); Brave: a body of at most 2 MB and 15,000 ms for a request, at least 1,100 ms between two requests of one key in a process (`braveSearch.ts`); see [web research](../infrastructure/web-research.md) |
| `read` actions per run | 12, successful or not; a page takes at most 20 seconds, 2 MB after decompression and 4 redirects |
| One model turn of the loop, the whole loop | 60 seconds, 5 minutes |
| Snippet, page excerpt | 1,500 characters, 8,000 characters |
| PDF: body, pages read, text, time, heap of the thread, files at once | 10,485,760 bytes, 100 pages, 320,000 characters, 20,000 ms, 256 MiB, 2 |
| PDF: inflated size of one stream, of all streams (counted before the library reads the file), growth of the process while the thread reads | 64 MiB, 128 MiB (at most half the growth allowed), 30% of the memory limit of the container between 64 and 1,024 MiB (1,024 MiB with no limit), or `VENDOR_RESEARCH_PDF_MAX_GROWTH_MB` |
| Page built by a script, read in the headless browser (off unless `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS` is `true`): requests, bytes, time, pages at once | 60, 12 MiB, 20,000 ms, 2 |
| Parts of a long page, parts kept as sources, text of a page source | 40 parts of at most 8,000 characters, 2 parts, 24,010 characters |
| Evidence pack | 100,000 bytes serialized; trimmed from the largest category, the records of the registers first, see [Research](#research) |
| Pages where the vendor states its terms | 8 documents for a run; a kept page is at most 4,000 lines and 100,000 characters (a line longer than 600 characters is kept as parts of at most 600); forgotten after 365 days |
| Source of the change of such a page | 6,000 characters, a line at most 600 |
| Model call of the analysis | 120 seconds, retries included |
| Lookup in the public databases (the CISA catalog, the NVD, Have I Been Pwned, the SEC), on the status page of the vendor and in the sanctions list | 8 seconds for the six together; at most 5, 3, 3, 3, 4 (a summary and 3 incidents for the status page) and 2 records; paid for by no budget |
| Reads to check the findings that rest on snippets | 2 pages for a finding, one deadline of 20 seconds for all the reads |
| Records of the vendor risk database offered to a run | 5 of the catalog; 3 of each of the NVD, the SEC, the CVE records, MITRE ATLAS, the NHTSA crash reports, the FDA recalls and the AI Incident Database; 2 of each sanctions list, of the Consolidated Screening List and of the FDA list; of the 23 lists of 2026-11-10, 2 of ten of them (`canada_sema`, `california_breaches`, `charlotin`, `ftc_penalties`, `eu_dg_comp`, `ftc_competition`, `co_sic_penalties`, `nhtsa_recalls`, `nhtsa_odi`, `fedramp`) and 1 of each of the other thirteen; a bundled list is stale 60 days after its bundle was built, a read-through answer 48 hours after it was fetched |
| Model call of the verifier | 30 seconds, retries included |
| Findings kept | 3 per category, 8 per run |
| Manual cooldown | `VENDOR_RESEARCH_MANUAL_COOLDOWN_MINUTES` (10), per vendor |
| Scheduled scan | Once an hour, at minute 11 (UTC); for each organization that chose an interval, at most 5 vendors and only while the runs of the last 24 hours are under half of the daily cap; at most 10 runs a pass over all organizations (2 for each place of the worker, at least 5), the first organization changing every hour; its waiting jobs are taken after manual and automatic runs |
| Daily cap | `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP` (50), per organization, rolling 24 hours, automatic and manual runs together, the started runs of a deleted vendor included |
| A stuck queued run | 15 minutes (`STALE_RUN_MINUTES`) |
| A run's own time limit | 20 minutes (`RUN_TIME_LIMIT_MINUTES`); the sweep fails a running run after 25 (`STALE_RUNNING_RUN_MINUTES`) |

- **Known limit: the memory of a rendered page.** What reaches the server process from the headless browser is bounded (the HTML is cut to 2 MiB inside the page), and so is the JavaScript heap of each page (256 MB). The rest of the browser's memory for a page (its nodes, its text, typed arrays) is not: only the 20 seconds of a render and the 2 pages at once bound it, so a page can still take the browser, and with it the container, past its memory limit (see [web research](../infrastructure/web-research.md)).
- **Known limit: a vendor whose own domain is a shared host.** A host where many have pages (`sharedHosts.ts`) is never the vendor's own, so a vendor whose website is exactly such a host (Hugging Face at `huggingface.co`, npm at `npmjs.com`) loses its own site (no page of the host is on the vendor's site, and its policy pages are not watched), the `domain:` key of its verified domain and the brand of its domain. Its Have I Been Pwned breaches and its status page count only when they name the vendor, though a vendor one of whose names is the brand of the host keeps every breach of the domain (see [Records of public databases](#records-of-public-databases)). That is the price of never taking a stranger's page on such a host for the vendor's.
- The cooldown applies to manual starts only, but it looks at the vendor's newest run of any trigger type, so an automatic run holds off a manual one. An automatic start does not wait for the cooldown; it is held to the daily cap. The same holds for a scheduled start, and the scan stops at half of the cap (see [The scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan)).
- Both limits are count queries over `vendor_research_runs` (the cooldown over the vendor's runs, the daily cap over the organization's, `countRunsSinceQuery`), and the daily cap adds the runs that started and no longer exist (below). They count every run except two kinds that did no research and called no model, so they count for neither:
  - a run that **failed before it started** (its job could not be queued, or it was swept as stuck while still `queued`). A start refused with 503 can therefore be tried again at once;
  - a run that is **still `queued` more than 15 minutes after it was created** (`STALE_RUN_MINUTES`, the limit of the stuck-run sweep below). Its job was lost, for example because Redis was emptied. (The backend used to empty the queue of the automations each time it started, `obliterate` in `scheduleVendorReviewDateNotification`, which took the waiting research jobs with it while they were on that queue. Research jobs now have a queue of their own, and no start empties a queue.) The sweep only looks at the vendor that is started or whose latest run is read, so without this rule such a run could use up a slot of the daily cap for the whole 24 hours. A worker that picks up the job of such a run later does not start it either (the start has the same 15-minute limit), so the run can only end as `timed out`, without a web request or a model call.

  A `running` run counts however long it has run, and so do a completed run and a run that failed after it started.

  The daily cap also counts the runs of a deleted vendor. Deleting a vendor deletes its runs, findings, sources and snapshots (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), and a count over `vendor_research_runs` alone would then forget runs that searched and called a model. So the statement that starts a run (`markRunRunningQuery`) also writes one row to `vendor_research_run_ledger`: the organization, the run's id and the run's `created_at`, and nothing of the vendor. `countRunsSinceQuery` adds to the runs that count the ledger rows of the window whose run no longer exists, so a run that exists is counted once, through its own row, and its status and age keep deciding. A started run of a deleted vendor counts until 24 hours after it was created. A run that never started (it failed before it started, or is still queued) spent nothing and has no ledger row, so deleting its vendor frees its slot, as before. The cooldown is per vendor and does not read the ledger. Known limit: a run that is running when its vendor is deleted is not stopped. It goes on until it ends, within its own time limit (20 minutes, `RUN_TIME_LIMIT_MINUTES`), and counts toward the cap.

  The ledger keeps a row at least 7 days (`LEDGER_RETENTION_DAYS`). Each start of a run also deletes at most 50 (`LEDGER_PRUNE_BATCH`) of that organization's rows older than that, through the index on `(organization_id, created_at)`. There is no job of its own, so an organization that starts no more runs keeps its last rows until its next start, and the rows go with the organization.
- A run that has been `queued` for more than 15 minutes, or `running` for more than 25 (counted from `started_at`, or from `created_at` if it never started), is failed with the error `timed out`. The 25 minutes are the run's own limit plus 5 minutes of grace: the pipeline fails a run that runs past `RUN_TIME_LIMIT_MINUTES` (20) itself, so the sweep only reaches a running run whose worker died, never one that is still working. The end of a run is guarded: the finish (`finishRunQuery`) writes only a run that is `queued` or `running`, so a run that the sweep failed is never overwritten by a late worker, and a run that ended while the pipeline was saving its findings loses those findings (the transaction rolls back) rather than the end. There is no background sweep: it runs before a new run is created and whenever the latest run is read. So a worker that is down leaves a run `queued` until the next start or read, and neither a crash nor a restart blocks a vendor or leaves the UI on "Researching" for ever.
- A queued run also waits for a free place on the research worker. Research has a queue of its own, `vendor-research`, and a worker of its own (`services/vendorResearch/queue.ts` and `worker.ts`), started with the other workers by `npm run worker` (`jobs/worker.ts`). The worker takes the runs of that queue only, `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` at a time for each worker process (5 unless set, see [Configuration](#configuration)), and a run can hold a place for up to about 7 minutes (5 for the research loop, 2 for the analysis, the checks after it half a minute). Runs and the other background jobs (notifications, recomputes, reports) no longer wait for each other. A burst of runs can still leave the last one queued past the 15-minute limit, which the sweep ends as `timed out`; this takes many runs started at once (for example many vendors created in a short time, each with an automatic run) or a worker that is down. The vendor then shows the run as `timed out`, which counts toward neither limit, and **Research again** starts a new one at once. The remedy for a burst is a higher setting or another worker process. The hourly scan is no such burst: it queues at most 10 runs a pass (2 for each place, see [The scheduled scan](#the-scheduled-scan)), and its waiting jobs are taken after the runs people start. The bound counts the places of one worker process; with several processes the scan uses only part of them.
- Each run costs at most 24 steps of the loop (each step is one structured call, which can be retried when the answer does not fit), the analysis call (more only when an answer has to be asked for again), one call of the verifier over the findings that stay, 8 searches and 12 page reads, and at most two more page reads for each finding that rests on snippets. A typical run is 8 to 16 steps, roughly 60,000 to 130,000 input tokens for the loop and 20,000 to 30,000 for the analysis: cents on a DeepSeek-class price, a dollar or more on a frontier model. These are estimates. The organization pays on its own key, and the daily cap bounds it, the started runs of a deleted vendor included (see above). `searches_used` and `pages_read` measure the tool side; the provider's usage page measures the model side.

### Accept and dismiss

`acceptFinding` and `dismissFinding` are in `services/vendorResearch/findings.service.ts`.

**Accept** checks the edits of the request (see [Vendor Research Endpoints](#vendor-research-endpoints)) and then is one transaction. A bad edit is answered 400 before anything is locked, read or written, except a framework that does not exist: whether each framework exists is checked inside the transaction (step 4), after the lock and the action owner, so that 400 can follow a 409 of a lock that was held. Every query of the accept, the lookup of the vendor included (`getVendorByIdQuery` takes an optional transaction), runs on the connection of that transaction.

1. The finding row is locked with `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` (organization and id). A lock that is not available means another accept, or the status job relabelling the finding, holds it at this moment, and it is answered 409 at once with "This finding is being updated. Try again in a moment." (the finding may still be pending, so it is not said to be decided): a request that waits holds a pooled connection while the lock holder still needs more, and accepts that waited could use up the pool between them. A retry can succeed if the other decision let go without deciding.
2. A missing finding, or one whose vendor is gone, is a 404. A finding that is not `pending` is a 409.
3. The action owner is resolved (below).
4. When the person picked frameworks, each of them must exist (`checkFrameworksExist`); one that does not is a 400 "Invalid frameworks", and nothing has been written.
5. The vendor risk is created with `createVendorRiskInTransaction` (`services/vendors/vendorRiskCreate.service.ts`): the insert into `vendorrisks` with the description, impact, likelihood, severity and action plan (each the edit of the request when there is one, else the finding's; no order number) and the risk level the insert computes from the likelihood and the severity, the frameworks the person picked (each checked to be a framework before the insert, so an unknown id is a 400 and never a failed insert that aborts the transaction), and the change-history record of the creation by the user, with the framework links. The vendor risk form and the Advisor create through `createVendorRisk` in `services/vendorRisk.service.ts`, which applies the same rules in a transaction of its own; `createVendorRiskInTransaction` exists because the accept writes the risk and the finding's decision in one transaction. The two are kept in step until they share one implementation. The vendor is the finding's own, whatever the request says.
6. The finding is marked `accepted`, with `accepted_vendor_risk_id`, `decided_by` and `decided_at`.

Anything that goes wrong rolls the whole accept back and leaves the finding `pending`. Only after the commit does `runVendorRiskPostCommitEffects` run, again the same as for the form: the related-risk recompute job, the candidate notice to the owners of project risks in the vendor's use cases, and the action owner's assignment notice, each fire-and-forget. A rolled-back accept announces nothing.

**Edits and atomicity.** The risk the person saw and edited is created and the finding is settled in the same transaction, so a risk is never created without its finding being accepted, nor a finding accepted without its risk. If anything fails (a framework that does not exist, a lock held by another decision), the finding stays `pending` and the form that sent the request stays open with what was typed.

**Default action owner.** With no `action_owner` in the request, the risk's owner is the vendor's assignee, but only if that person is a user of the organization; otherwise the risk has no owner. Nothing in the database ties an assignee to the vendor's organization, and an owner from another organization would be sent a notice with the risk's text and the vendor's name. The accept goes on without an owner and a log line with ids says why. `action_owner: null` means nobody, and a number must be a user of the organization.

**Dismiss** is one conditional update (`WHERE status = 'pending'`), so a finding is dismissed once and never after it was accepted. The reason is one of `not_applicable`, `already_covered`, `inaccurate`, `accepted_risk`, `wrong_vendor`, `outdated` and `other`. `wrong_vendor` and `outdated` are the two that blame the research and not the risk: the sources were about another company, or too old. Every decision is also counted by a metric (see [Decision statistics](#vendor-research-endpoints)). The note is optional: `null`, an empty string and blanks all mean no note, and a note is cleaned of control characters and trimmed before it is counted against the 500-character limit. The finding keeps its wording, which is what stops similar candidates from coming back (see [De-duplication](#de-duplication)).

### Notice to the assignee

After an **automatic** or a **scheduled** run that stored at least one finding, the vendor's assignee (when the vendor has one, and that person is a user of the vendor's organization) gets a `vendor_research_findings` in-app notification that links to `/vendors/research-findings?findingsVendorId=<vendor id>`. The organization check is the one the accept uses for the default action owner (`findUsersNotInOrganization`). The vendor create and update of the API refuse a new assignee who is not a user of the organization (see [Vendor Endpoints](#vendor-endpoints)), but the stored assignee can still be one: a user who has since left the organization (an assignee who is not changed is not checked again), a vendor stored before that check, or one written by a writer that does not check (the advisor's vendor functions, `services/vendor.service.ts`, the demo driver). For such a person no notification is written, nothing is raised, the run is unaffected, and one debug line with ids only is logged. A manual run does not notify: the person who started it is looking at the result. A scheduled run notifies for the same reason an automatic one does: nobody started it, so nobody is looking. The notification type, link, metadata and dedup index are described in [Notifications](./notifications.md#vendor-research-notice).

### Safety

- **Web text is untrusted.** It enters the prompts inside a delimited block with its markup characters escaped. The model of the loop can only name a search or an address, and code decides what happens (next bullet); the analysis has no actions, its answer is bound to a schema and then checked by code against the retrieved text, and nothing is written to the register unless a person accepts. The findings section says they are AI-suggested and must be verified, and the UI shows finding text, quotes and model notes as plain text, never as markup.
- **Email.** The text of an accepted finding reaches the action owner's assignment email like any risk text. That email HTML-escapes the names it shows (the mail compiler escapes every plain value) and the context it shows (the service escapes each value with `escapeHtml` of `utils/sanitization.utils.ts`; see [Notifications](./notifications.md#assignment-emails)), so text that a model wrote after reading a web page shows as typed and cannot add a tag, a link or an image.
- **Links.** A source is a link only when its URL is `http` or `https`; it opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. The server drops any other URL from the evidence before the model sees it.
- **The server fetches pages the model chose, behind a guard.** That is a server-side request forgery surface, so every request goes through `guardedFetch` of the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-page-guard). Only `http` and `https` on ports 80 and 443 are used, and never an IP address, a URL with credentials, `localhost` or a name that is reserved for private networks. Every address the host resolves to must be public, checked on the connection itself, so DNS cannot answer differently afterwards. Redirects are followed by hand (4 at most) and each hop is checked again, and a redirect from https to http is refused. A page may be read only if a search of the run returned its address or it is on the vendor's domain, so a page cannot send the agent to an arbitrary address. The body is cut at 2 MB after decompression, and the whole fetch at 20 seconds. The bot says who it is (`VerifyWiseResearchBot`), sends no cookie, credential or `Referer`, and obeys each site's `robots.txt` for pages.
- **The search key is optional.** The search needs no account and no key: an Anthropic key searches with Claude on the key it has, and every other key with the GDELT Project, which needs none. An organization may add a Brave Search key, which a key with no search of its own then searches with: it is stored encrypted (`vendor_research_settings.brave_api_key_encrypted`), never returned (the search-key endpoints answer `configured` and `last4`), never logged and never put in an error, a trace or the run's spec when that is copied, and Brave's text is never stored (see [Brave text is never stored](#brave-text-is-never-stored)). The LLM key is the organization's own: it is never returned (the config endpoint returns booleans) and never logged. The pipeline logs ids only (organization, vendor and run), never page text, URLs, queries, model output or keys, and the errors of the page guard and of the search are fixed phrases that repeat nothing of the URL or the query.
- **Stored errors are fixed phrases.** A run is served to every role, so its `error` is always one of: `Vendor not found`; `No LLM key configured`; `Model call failed: HTTP <status>`, `... timed out`, `... the answer did not match the expected format` or `... unexpected error` (from the loop or from the analysis); `The run stopped because the web could not be reached`; `The model did not use the research actions`; `Unexpected error`; `Saving the findings failed`; `timed out` (written by the sweep, and by the pipeline for a run that reaches its time limit); `could not enqueue`. The message of a model or provider error is neither stored nor logged by the pipeline (the log line has the error's name and HTTP status), and the message of any other error, which can name a host, a port or a query, goes to the server log only. The client maps this closed set to translated sentences and shows a generic one for anything else.
- **Category notes.** The note of a category is a fixed phrase from the set under [Category outcomes](#category-outcomes), except for a `nothing_found` category in which no search or page failed and the model proposed nothing: there it may be the model's own text (200 characters at most). The UI shows that one as plain text under "AI note (unverified)". An `over_cap` category has only its fixed note, and the UI drops any other note on it.
- **Data sent out.** During a run the server requests `api.search.brave.com` for a key that is not Anthropic's when the organization has a Brave key, and for an Anthropic key after its own search was refused (each search a GET with the organization's Brave key and the normalized query: the vendor's name and topic words), `api.gdeltproject.org` for a key that is not Anthropic's without a Brave key, or after Brave refused the key (one request: the vendor's names and the topic words of the news categories), searches through the provider of the research key for an Anthropic key (the queries the model writes: by its prompt the vendor's name and topic words, which the code does not enforce), and public pages, through the [page guard](../infrastructure/web-research.md#the-page-guard): those on any host that a search returned, and those on the vendor's own domain and its subdomains, which its pages link to (a redirect can lead a request on to another public host, which the guard checks again). Every request names the bot. The LLM provider, which the organization chose, receives the vendor's name, its domain and what the vendor provides, the queries, the snippets and page text that the research read, and, in the analysis only, the vendor's scorecard answers on data sensitivity, business criticality and regulatory exposure. When an Admin chose another key for the analysis, each provider receives only its own part: the provider of the research key the loop's calls, the provider of the analysis key the vendor, the scorecard answers and the evidence. No other organization data leaves. The bundled lists are downloaded by maintainers when they build a release, never by the server, and nothing about any vendor goes to their hosts; a read-through source is asked by one key of the vendor (GLEIF by its stored LEI, a public identifier, and by nothing else). An installation that cannot reach the web cannot use the feature fully: a run that finds no record in the public databases and whose calls the web never answers fails with "The run stopped because the web could not be reached" (a run that found records analyses them and completes).
- **Stored third-party text.** Page excerpts and snippets are kept in `vendor_research_runs.evidence` as an audit trail (up to 100 KB per run). The UI shows only the quotes (300 characters) and links. Pruning old evidence is out of scope.

### Permissions and multi-tenancy

- **Permissions.** Starting a run, accepting and dismissing need `vendor.edit` (`authorize("vendor.edit")`, the key of the vendor and vendor risk writes, so a role that cannot add a vendor risk by hand cannot add one by accepting a finding; Admin and Editor by default). The trace, the decision statistics and the settings need `llmKeys.admin`. Everything else, including the findings list, the latest run and the config, needs only a signed-in user, so every role can see what research found.
- **Tenant isolation.** Every query filters on `organization_id`. The organization and the user always come from the request, never from the body or the path. The `vendor_id` of a run or a finding is a foreign key to `vendors` only and is not tied to an organization in the database, so every id from a path is looked up in the caller's organization before it is used, and a vendor or finding of another organization is a 404, the same as one that does not exist. The job payload carries `organizationId`. The seven tenant tables of the research (`vendor_research_runs`, `vendor_research_settings`, `vendor_research_sources`, `vendor_research_snapshots`, `vendor_research_run_ledger`, `vendor_vrdb_matches` and `vendor_risk_findings`) are in the tenant-isolation registry with isolation tests and carry the `tenant_isolation` policy (the ledger's from its own migration), the settings of one organization cannot name the key of another (a 404), and the scan reads the vendors and the interval of one organization at a time. The scan's first statement, `listScanOrganizationsQuery`, is the one exception: it lists the organizations that turned the scan on, with their intervals, across organizations (see `docs/technical/security/tenant-isolation.md`, §4.3).

### Configuration

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_AUTO_RUN` | `true` | Research a vendor automatically after it is created. Only `false` (any case) turns it off; people can still start a run by hand |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_MANUAL_COOLDOWN_MINUTES` | `10` | Minutes before the same vendor can be researched again by hand. `0` means no wait. Anything but a whole number of 0 or more falls back to 10 |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP` | `50` | Runs one organization may start in a rolling 24 hours, automatic and manual together; the runs of a deleted vendor that started still count. Anything but a whole number of 1 or more falls back to 50 |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` | `5` | Runs one worker process carries out at the same time. Anything but a whole number of 1 or more falls back to 5 |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS` | `false` | Open a page that only a script can build in a headless Chromium, so that its text can be read (see [Rendering a page that a script builds](../infrastructure/web-research.md#rendering-a-page-that-a-script-builds)). Only `true` (any case) turns it on. A process that runs as root never starts the browser (`RootRenderRefused`), and the published image runs as root, so it takes effect only where the backend and the worker run as an unprivileged user |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` | `true` | Look the vendor up in the [vendor risk database](#vendor-risk-database) (the bundled copies of the lists, the read-through of the NVD, the SEC and GLEIF) and in the public databases that a run asks itself (Have I Been Pwned and the status page of the vendor; see [Records of public databases](#records-of-public-databases)). Only `false` (any case) turns it all off: no bundle is loaded, nothing is looked up, and the coverage of a run says the databases are turned off. Both the backend and the worker read it |
| `VENDOR_RESEARCH_PDF_MAX_GROWTH_MB` | 30% of the container's memory limit | How much a worker process may grow, in MiB, while it reads one PDF; a read that takes it further is ended (see [Limits](#limits)). A positive whole number; any other value is ignored, with one line in the log. Unset, it is 30% of the memory limit of the container, between 64 and 1,024 MiB, and 1,024 MiB with no limit |

The seven variables are optional and are listed in `Servers/.env.example`. `VENDOR_RESEARCH_VRDB`, which turned the vendor risk database on while it was tested, is gone: the database is always on and nothing reads that variable, so a deployment that still sets it changes nothing. They are read from the process environment each time they are used, not at import. There is no search variable: research needs an LLM key saved for the organization, which an Admin adds in the LLM Keys section of Settings → API Keys, and nothing else. The Brave Search key is a setting of the organization, not of the deployment: an Admin adds it in the **Search API key** section of the same page. Without an LLM key the feature reports "not set up" and nothing runs. Every container that runs a worker process also needs outbound internet access (see [Two worker processes](#two-worker-processes) below): HTTPS to `api.gdeltproject.org` (a key that is not Anthropic's searches the news there when the organization has no Brave key; an Anthropic key searches through its provider), to `api.search.brave.com` for an organization with a Brave key (the API process also asks it once to check a key when an Admin saves one), to the public databases and their hosts (unless `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` is `false`; the list is in the [web research toolkit](../infrastructure/web-research.md#what-leaves-the-server) and in `docs/deployment/CONFIGURATION_REFERENCE.md`) and to public websites (and HTTP on port 80 for the sites that only offer that).

**The scheduled scan has no variable.** It is a setting of the organization (**Research settings**), it runs on the same queue and workers as the runs, and the backend registers its hourly job at start (`jobs/producer.ts`). Its limits are the batch of 5, half of `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP`, and twice `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` (at least 5) runs a pass over all organizations.

**The worker needs no search settings.** The API process of the backend reads the first three variables, for the config endpoint, the automatic start, the cooldown and the daily cap. A worker process reads `VENDOR_RESEARCH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY`, for the number of runs it carries out at once and the size of a pass of the scan, `VENDOR_RESEARCH_HEADLESS`, for the browser that it opens a page built by a script in, `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS`, for the lookup in the public databases, `VENDOR_RESEARCH_PDF_MAX_GROWTH_MB`, at its first PDF, and `VENDOR_RESEARCH_DAILY_RUN_CAP`, for the half of the cap that its scan may use: it takes the organization's LLM key from the database and runs the pipeline. It needs outbound internet access, though. A worker that cannot reach the web ends a run as `failed` with `The run stopped because the web could not be reached` when no call of the loop was answered and nothing is left to analyse; records of the public databases that the run found are analysed even when the web is down. `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS` is read by both processes: at start the backend queues the load of the bundles while it is on (`scheduleVendorRiskDatabaseLoad`), and the worker runs the load, checking it again, and looks the vendor up in the copies. Set it the same on the backend and on the worker.

#### Two worker processes

The backend container runs a worker as well. The image's command, `Servers/start.sh`, starts `node dist/jobs/worker.js` in the background and then the API, and the development backend runs `npm run watch`, which starts both too. The worker container (`docker-compose.yml`, `kubernetes/worker-deployment.yaml`, the Helm chart's `worker-deployment.yaml`) runs only the worker. So every deployment has at least two worker processes (the Kubernetes manifests and the Helm chart run two backend replicas, so three), and any of them may take a research run, the hourly scan or the load of the bundles. Each process reads its own environment and makes its requests from its own container. Set every vendor research variable to the same value on the backend and on the worker, and open the outbound access for both: a value set on one container applies only to the jobs that container takes (with `VENDOR_RESEARCH_RECORDS=false` on the backend only, no load is queued but a run that the worker container takes still asks the public databases), and a container that cannot reach the web fails the runs it happens to take. In the backend container the research shares the memory limit with the API (512Mi in the Kubernetes manifests and the Helm chart, as for the worker). Whether the backend container should stop starting a worker when a separate worker is deployed is an open decision; today it always starts one.

The Kubernetes, Helm and Ansible templates do not set the vendor research variables, so the seven take their defaults unless a value is added to the environment of both the backend and the worker.

| Deployment | What to do |
|------------|------------|
| `docker-compose.prod.yml` | Nothing beyond setting the variables in `.env.prod`: the file hands the whole `.env.prod` to the backend and the worker |
| `docker-compose.override.yml` (development stack) | It gives `.env.dev` to the backend but not to the worker, so the worker container keeps the defaults while the worker process of the backend container reads `.env.dev`. To try a value other than the default, add it to the worker's `environment` in `docker-compose.yml` as well, or stop the worker container |
| `kubernetes/backend-deployment.yaml`, `kubernetes/worker-deployment.yaml` | These list variables by name and do not set the vendor research variables. Both containers run a worker process, so a value other than the default has to be added to both deployments (or to `kubernetes/configmap.yaml`, with a `configMapKeyRef` to it in each deployment, since they read the config map key by key) |
| Helm chart: `kubernetes/helm/verifywise/templates/backend-deployment.yaml`, `worker-deployment.yaml`, `configmap.yaml`, `values.yaml` | Same: the chart does not set them. A value other than the default has to be added to both deployment templates (or to the config map and the values, with a `configMapKeyRef` in each deployment template) |
| Ansible: `ansible/templates/.env.j2` | Renders `.env.prod`, which compose hands to both processes. The template does not set them; add the lines for the values you want to change |

Network egress is part of the deployment. The research runs in the worker processes, one in the worker container and one in each backend container (see [Two worker processes](#two-worker-processes)), and its requests are made directly from that process (the load of the bundles makes none): the page guard reads no proxy setting and resolves the public host names itself. A firewall or network policy that blocks outbound HTTPS from either container, or a network whose only way out is a proxy, makes every run that finds no record in the public databases fail with "The run stopped because the web could not be reached" (a run that found records analyses them and completes).

## Change History

All vendor changes are tracked:

| Field | Description |
|-------|-------------|
| entity_type | "vendor" or "vendor_risk" |
| entity_id | Vendor or risk ID |
| action | created/updated/deleted |
| changed_by | User ID |
| field_name | Changed field |
| old_value | Previous value |
| new_value | New value |
| timestamp | When changed |

Besides the form fields, vendor history records use case link changes ("Use
cases", diffed from the links stored before and after the write). It records
the number of linked use cases, not their titles: every vendor viewer reads the
history, and most can't see every linked use case. And
vendor risk history records moving a risk to another vendor ("Vendor") and
framework mapping changes ("Frameworks"). Link and framework lists compare as
sets, so a reorder records nothing. User and vendor names are looked
up within the organization: a user from another organization shows as
`User #id`.

## Frontend Structure

### Main Page

**Location:** `pages/Vendors/`

**Features:**
- Four route-driven tabs (see **Tabs of the page** below): Vendors, Risks, Research findings, Insights
- Vendor list with search/filter/group
- Vendor risks tab: the register, its toolbar and summary cards
- Add/edit vendor modal
- Add/edit risk modal
- Export functionality
- Page tour for guidance

**Tabs of the page.** The tabs are route-driven (`TAB_PATHS` and `tabOfPath` in `pages/Vendors/tabs.ts`, which also holds `TAB_HEADERS` and `TAB_TEXT`): the active tab is read from the path, without regard to case, to a trailing slash and to percent-encoding, as the router matches it (`/Vendors/Risks/` and `/vendors/%72isks` are the Risks route), and choosing a tab navigates to its path, so every tab can be linked to. All four routes render the same `Vendors` page (`application/config/routes.tsx`), so the page's state (the heat map cell filter among it) survives a change of tab. The `TableFilter` of the Vendors toolbar and of the Risks toolbar keeps its own state inside and are mounted only while their tab is open, so the page passes each of them its conditions, logic, grouping and sort order as defaults every time they mount.

| Tab | Path | Count | Content |
|-----|------|-------|---------|
| Vendors | `/vendors` | the vendors listed | The vendor list and its toolbar |
| Risks | `/vendors/risks` | the vendor risks listed | The register of vendor risks, its toolbar (**Risk status**, filter, group, columns, search, export, **Add new risk**) and the summary cards. A `HeatCellNotice` stands above the register while a heat map cell filters it |
| Research findings | `/vendors/research-findings` | the pending findings of every vendor | `VendorResearchFindings` (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)) |
| Insights | `/vendors/insights` | none | `VendorRiskInsights` (see [Vendor risk insights](#vendor-risk-insights)) |

The header (title and description, `TAB_HEADERS`) follows the tab, and the tab names and tooltips are `TAB_TEXT`; the strings have de, fr and es keys in `i18n/modules/vendors.ts` (`Vendors/__tests__/tabs.test.ts` fails when one is missing), and `npm run i18n:audit:strict` must stay at 0 gaps. That audit reads a string only up to its first apostrophe and only in the attribute itself, so `src/i18n/__tests__/pageHeaders.translations.test.ts` reads the title, description and subtitle of every page header (`PageHeaderExtended`, `PageHeader`, `PageTitle`, `GovernanceLayout`) as code and fails when one has no de, fr or es key; a description that is built from data (`Manage users for organization #{orgId}`) is written as a template, translated with `t()` and filled with `fill()`. The description under the title is `TabDescription`: the four descriptions differ in length and wrap, so it lays them in one cell of a grid and shows the open tab's, which keeps the tab bar under it at the same height on every tab (it moved by a line at 1024 px when each description was rendered alone). A path that is none of the last three is the Vendors tab. The page's `TabBar` is `scrollable`, so the four tabs scroll with arrows when they do not fit. The `?vendorId=` and `?riskId=` parameters name a link that opens that vendor's or risk's form over the page. A link is taken up once (`handledLink`), and let go of when the URL no longer names one, which every outcome of the read ends in, so the same link followed a second time (Wise Search to the same vendor again) opens it again; the query is cleared whether or not the vendor came back. The reads are numbered (`linkReads`), and the answer of a read that a later link replaced is dropped: reads are not answered in the order they were asked, and a slow earlier link would otherwise open its vendor over the later link's, or move the reader back to the Vendors tab. The `?riskId=` parameter (the activity link `/vendors/risks?riskId=N`, and Wise Search's `/vendors?tab=risks&riskId=N`) opens that risk's form and ends on `/vendors/risks` with the query cleared: the page navigates to the Risks tab, with `replace`, instead of clearing the search parameters from the path it was on. A `?findingsVendorId=` on `/vendors/risks` (a notification stored before the findings had a tab) is sent to `/vendors/research-findings`. The tab panels mount only the active tab, so the findings list and the latest run (Research findings tab) and the insight reports (Insights tab, one per sub-tab) are read when their tab is first shown; the vendor list, the register and the vendor lists the findings need are read by the page itself and stay mounted across tabs, as does the count of the Research findings tab (`usePendingFindingsCount`), so that it shows on every tab. The breadcrumbs name the three paths (`presentation/components/breadcrumbs/routeMapping.ts`, static entries that win over the dynamic `/vendors/<slug>` pattern, which would say "Vendor details"): `/vendors/risks` is "Vendor risks", `/vendors/research-findings` is "Research findings" and `/vendors/insights` is "Insights". The tab bar's icons come from `presentation/utils/lucideIconMap.ts`, which lists the icons by name (`Copy`, `Grid3x3` and `Radar`, which the Insights sub-tabs name, were added to it).

### Lists, filters and exports

`GET /vendorRisks/all` answers one row per (vendor risk, use case of its
vendor). `useVendorRisks` and the dashboard make the rows distinct by risk id
first (`distinctVendorRisks`, `application/utils/vendorRiskRows.ts`), keeping
each risk's `project_ids` and `project_titles`, so counts, cards, the heat map,
tables and exports show each risk once. The use case filter matches any of a
risk's use cases.

Each tab has its own use case filter: the Vendors tab's narrows the vendor
list only, the Risks tab's the risks only. The Vendors tab's risk counts are
the vendor's active risks, whatever the Risks tab filters. Filter options use
the stored enum values (data sensitivity, business criticality, severity) and
the card level labels ("No risk" to "Very high").

The risk export is the risks on screen (search, filters, card, heat map cell),
once each, with use case names. The vendor export adds use cases and risk
counts, and formats the review date with the table's formatter. A search or
filter that hides every row shows "No vendors (vendor risks) match your search
or filters." instead of the first-run empty state, and both tables move back to
the last page with rows when the list shrinks.

### Components

| Component | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|
| `AddNewVendor` (`components/Modals/NewVendor/`) | Create/edit vendor modal. The review date starts empty and can be cleared; a failed save keeps the form as typed |
| `AddNewRisk` (`components/Modals/NewRisk/`) | Create/edit risk modal (Risk details, Custom fields, Activity, Linked risks); `initialTab="linked-risks"` opens an existing risk on its links. Given a `preview` (`VendorRiskPreview`) it is the **draft** of a research finding: pre-filled, vendor fixed, and its Save hands the form to `preview.onSave` instead of creating the risk itself (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)). With no vendor it won't save ("Add a vendor first…"); `vendor_id` and `action_owner` are sent as numbers; a failed save keeps the form |
| `VendorRiskLinksPanel` | The Linked risks tab: child project risks and related vendor risks, plus Suggest children and the related scan |
| `RelateVendorRiskForm` | Picker that relates another vendor risk to this one |
| `InlineFormCard` | Bordered frame, title and Cancel/primary actions for the forms that open inside the Linked risks tab |
| `VendorRiskInsights` (`pages/Vendors/VendorRiskInsights/`) | Content of the Insights tab: heat map, blast radius, duplicate and coverage sub-tabs. `HeatCellNotice` is the notice above the register on the Risks tab while a heat map cell filters it |
| `VendorResearchFindings` (`pages/Vendors/VendorResearchFindings/`) | Content of the Research findings tab: pending findings with Preview suggested risk and Dismiss, and the latest research run of a vendor (see [Vendor research](#vendor-research)) |
| `VendorResearchButton` (`components/VendorResearchButton/`) | **Research risks** icon button in each row of the vendors table |
| `RiskTable` (`components/Table/RisksTable/`) | Display vendor risks, including the Inherited by column |
| `TableWithPlaceholder` (`components/Table/WithPlaceholder/`) | Main vendor list |
| `TableFilter` (`components/Table/TableFilter.tsx`, with `useFilterBy`) | Dynamic filtering (`FilterBy` in the classic look, filter chips in phase 1) |

### Hooks

| Hook | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `useVendors()` (`hooks/useVendors.ts`) | Fetch vendors |
| `useDeleteVendor()` (`hooks/useVendors.ts`) | Delete mutation |
| `useVendorRisks()` (`hooks/useVendorRisks.tsx`, default export) | Fetch risks, distinct by risk id |
| `useDeleteVendorRisk()` (`hooks/useVendorRiskMutations.ts`) | Delete risk mutation; save and restore are there too |
| `useVendorExposure()`, `useVendorDuplicateCandidates()`, `useVendorFrameworkCoverage()` | Vendor risk insight reports (`hooks/useRiskLinks.ts`) |
| `useSuggestVendorRiskHierarchy(id)` | Suggest children on one vendor risk |
| `useRecomputeVendorRiskLinks()` | Scan every vendor risk for related vendor risks (Admin) |
| `useVendorResearchConfig()` | Whether research is set up (`hooks/useVendorResearch.ts`, as are the rows below) |
| `useVendorRiskFindingPages(filter)` | The findings list (one status, one vendor or all), read in pages of the server's cap; see the findings section below |
| `usePendingFindingsCount(refreshWhen)` | The number of pending findings over every vendor, for the count beside the Research findings tab: one row is asked for (`limit` 1) and only the total read, with the global error toast off. `undefined` until it is first read, and when that first read fails (no count is shown); after a failed refetch the previous count stays. It is read again when the open tab changes (the page passes the tab as `refreshWhen`), when the window regains the focus (`refetchOnWindowFocus`), after every decision, and when a run that the findings tab is watching ends (the invalidation of `FINDINGS_KEY`); deleting a vendor invalidates `VENDOR_RESEARCH_KEY` (`useDeleteVendor`). It does not poll, so a run that finishes while nothing watches it (one that started by itself, by schedule, or from the vendors table) shows in the count at the next change of tab or the next window focus, not at once |
| `useLatestVendorResearch(vendorId)` | The latest run of a vendor, polled while it is queued or running, and read again on window focus while it is active. `restartPolling()` starts a new two-minute window and reads the run at once |
| `useStartVendorResearch()` | Start a run |
| `useAcceptFinding()`, `useDismissFinding()` | Decide a finding. Accept takes the body of `AcceptFindingBody`: the owner and the edits of the form |
| `followResearch(queryClient, vendorId)`, `useFollowedResearchVendor()`, `useFollowedResearchVendorWhileOpen(onFollow)` | Leave the hint that makes the Research findings tab follow a run started on the vendors table; read it once when the tab mounts; and hear of a start that is answered while the tab is already open |

### Vendor research

**Findings section.** `pages/Vendors/VendorResearchFindings/` is the content of the Research findings tab, mounted by `pages/Vendors/index.tsx` at `/vendors/research-findings` (the tab panel mounts it only while that tab is shown). The tab is named "Research findings" with a count of the pending findings of every vendor (`usePendingFindingsCount`), and the page header carries its title and description. It shows pending findings only: 200 at a time: with every vendor listed, a note says how many are shown and to choose a vendor to see the rest; with one vendor chosen, **Load more** under the list reads the next 200 (`useVendorRiskFindingPages`, offset paging), and accepted or dismissed findings are not listed. **Load more** is busy while any read of the list is on its way (a refresh after a decision or a finished run reads every page again, and a next page asked for then would cancel it). The list can change between two pages without this screen deciding anything (another person decides a finding, a scheduled run adds some in front); when the pages read disagree (their totals differ, or a finding is on two of them), every page is read again from the start, at most 3 times in a row, and whether more is left is told by the findings held, each once, against the total. A change that adds and removes as many findings in front of the next page is not seen until the next refresh.

- Not collapsible: the findings are the content of the tab. The top of the tab says "AI-suggested from web sources. Verify before accepting." (the line is the last focus target described below). The former "Pending: N" chip is the count of the tab, and is the total over every vendor, whereas the summary box counts the vendor chosen.
- A **Vendor** filter ("All vendors" or one vendor). It starts from the **`findingsVendorId`** URL parameter (digits only, above zero; anything else is ignored), and when the URL has none, from the vendor of a run followed from the vendors table (see **Following a run**); with neither it starts on all vendors. The parameter is the one that the assignee notification links to: `/vendors/research-findings?findingsVendorId=<vendor id>`. A link stored before the findings had a tab of their own, `/vendors/risks?findingsVendorId=<vendor id>`, is redirected by the page (`navigate` with `replace`, the rest of the query string kept) to the same query on `/vendors/research-findings`. A new value in the URL while the page is mounted is followed. The existing `vendorId` parameter opens the vendor modal, so the section has a parameter of its own.
- **Summary.** Above the list, a box says what is pending in plain words. It is counted from the pending rows by `summarizeFindings` (`application/utils/vendorResearchList.ts`) and worded by `application/utils/vendorResearchPlainText.ts`: "16 possible risks to review. 10 are high risk." (High and Very High count as high; "All are high risk." when all are, and no second sentence when none is) and "Most are about: Legal and regulatory (7), Security incidents (5)." (the two categories with the most findings; left out when the findings are of one category). When the server holds more findings than it sent, the box only counts them, since the rest would describe a part of them. With a vendor chosen, the same box holds the run of that vendor (`RunSummary`, below). The box is not shown when nothing is pending and no vendor is chosen.
- **Groups and order.** The findings stand in groups (`groupFindings`): with a vendor chosen, under the name of their category, in the order of the categories; with all vendors, under the name of their vendor, and each row then names its category in a chip. A group heading shows the number of its findings, and each group is a list labelled by its heading inside the one list "Pending research findings". Inside a group the rows are ordered by risk level, highest first, then by credibility, then newest first (`orderFindings`). The focus after a decision follows the order on screen.
- Each row (`FindingRow`) is one line until it is opened: a button that holds a chevron, the risk level chip and the risk text (two lines at most while the row is closed); under it the credibility line and the cautions of the finding (see **Credibility**), and the **Dismiss** and **Preview** buttons. A click of the risk, or Enter or Space on it, opens the details (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`), which are rendered only while the row is open: the impact under "Why it matters to you"; the action plan under "What to do" (accepting writes it to the register, so the person who decides can read all three texts that will be written, and the form of **Preview** shows them again to change; nothing is shown for a finding without one); under "Why this risk level" the rationale (plain text; a finding without one, which is the case for the findings of runs from before the sentence existed, has the caption with the next line alone) and the line "Severity: Major · Likelihood: Likely", which stands where each scale used to have a chip; and the sources: the host as a link (`http` and `https` only), the publication date, the tags of the source (see **Credibility**) and the quote as plain text. The texts are plain text like the quote. The **Preview** and **Dismiss** buttons of a row are described by that row's risk text (`aria-describedby`), so a screen reader says which risk a button decides.
- **Preview suggested risk** and **Dismiss** are hidden for users without `vendor.edit`, who see the section read-only. There is no one-click accept: a finding is added to the register through the vendor risk form, so the person sees, and can change, what will be written.
- **Preview suggested risk** opens `AddNewRisk` (the vendor risk form) on the finding, in place on the Research findings tab: nothing is requested and nothing is navigated to. The section passes a `VendorRiskPreview` (`domain/interfaces/i.vendor.ts`): a title ("Review suggested risk"), a description, the finding's `values` and an `onSave`. The form opens filled in with the finding's risk, impact, action plan, likelihood and severity (the register's top likelihood, `Almost certain`, is matched without regard to case); the vendor is fixed to the finding's; the **owner** starts on the vendor's assignee when that user belongs to the organization, else on the person adding the risk (`defaultActionOwner`), so the form can be saved as it opens; the risk level is the form's own, worked out from likelihood and severity as for any vendor risk (it can differ from the level chip on the card, which is the research's matrix: the form's level is the one saved); frameworks can be picked, and the custom fields of vendor risks are there, required ones included. A preview does not check the role again: the section only offers it to roles with `vendor.edit`. The section and its preview form read the organization's whole vendor list (`useVendors({ projectId: "all" })` in `pages/Vendors/index.tsx`), not the list that the Vendors tab's use case filter narrows, since the findings are not filtered by use case: a preview starts on its vendor and on that vendor's assignee whatever the filter. The page's own vendor risk form reads the same list, for a new risk and for an existing one (opened from the register table, from **Open risk** after an accept, or through the `riskId` URL parameter), and is told while it loads (`vendorsLoading`), so its Vendor field lists all the organization's vendors and an existing risk always shows its own vendor.
- **Save** in the form calls `onSave`, which sends the accept request with the form's values (`toAcceptBody` in `application/utils/vendorResearchPreview.ts`: the owner as a number, the texts, the register's likelihood and severity, the level, the frameworks; no vendor). The risk is created and the finding settled in one transaction (see [Accept and dismiss](#accept-and-dismiss)). The form then saves the values of the custom fields on the new risk and closes, the row goes, and a toast ("Risk added to the register") and a notice with an **Open risk** button for the new risk are shown. If the custom field values cannot be saved, the form still closes (saving again would add the risk twice) and a warning says to open the risk and try again. **Cancel**, or closing the form any other way, changes nothing: the finding is still pending. A request that fails leaves the form open with everything that was typed, so that it can be saved again; the global error toast reports the failure.
- **Dismiss** opens `DismissFindingModal` (a `StandardModal`) with a required reason and an optional note of up to 500 characters, counted in code points as the server counts them (most emoji are one, a flag or a skin-tone emoji are two), and announces "Finding dismissed." when it is done.
- After a decision, focus moves to the first button of the row that takes the decided row's place: the next row, else the previous one, else the line that says nothing is left ("No pending research findings." or, when research is not set up, "Vendor research is not set up.", both with `tabIndex={-1}`), so that a screen reader says the new state, else the line at the top of the tab ("AI-suggested from web sources. Verify before accepting."). Focus moves only if it has fallen to the page, so it never pulls a user back who has moved on.
- A decision that the server refuses is settled once the list has been read again. If the finding has left the pending list (404 for a finding or vendor that is gone, or 409 for a finding decided elsewhere), the dismiss modal or the vendor risk form of that finding closes by itself and focus moves as after a decision. This holds for the modal's finding whichever decision was answered last (two answers can arrive together, for example the refusal of one dismissal and the success of an accept of another finding), except while a request about that finding is still on its way: then its own answer decides. If the finding is still pending (the 409 of a lock held by another accept or the status job, `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`), the modal stays open with the reason and the note, and a retry can succeed. The global error toast skips 404 on purpose, so a 404 on accept or dismiss shows the warning "This finding no longer exists." through `onNotify`.
- **Credibility.** Every finding shows one line beside the caption "Credibility" (`credibilityLine` in `vendorResearchPlainText.ts`; there is no credibility chip, and no confidence chip or fallback): the sentence of the rule that decided the label, which starts with the label ("High: CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities lists it (CVE-2026-28001), as of 7 Oct 2026."), with the day of a copy that was out of date. The sentence is worded from `credibility_basis` by `credibilityRationale` in `application/utils/vendorResearchText.ts`, in the person's language, with the client's own names of the databases; the server's `credibility_rationale` is not shown. The client keeps its own copy of the ten lists that give context only, so that the line reads as the server's does: a finding that cites only records of them reads the sentence "Low: only registers that list the vendor are cited, and a register entry is not evidence of a risk." (translated), and such a record is left out of the sentence of a withdrawn record and of the day of a stale copy. A label with no basis, or with a rule the client does not know, stands alone ("Medium"). A label the backfill rated again says "Rated again with the current rule." only in an open row, since that is true of every older row once the labels have been recomputed. A finding whose basis rule is `legacy_confidence` (a finding of a run from before the rules, until the backfill) has its label with an asterisk and its sources counted, "Medium*: one source reports it (news.example.com)." or "Medium*: 3 sources report it (a.example, b.example)."; what the asterisk stands for is said once above the list ("* Earlier estimate: 13 of these findings were rated before the current rules for rating sources.", `earlierLegend`), and the row carries the words "(Earlier estimate)" for screen readers only. Under the line a row shows its cautions (`rowCautions`), each a sentence with a warning icon, on screen without a click: "Based on search results only: it could not be confirmed on the source pages." for a `snippet_only` finding (not said again when the rule of the label is `capped_unconfirmed` or `contradicted`, whose sentence says it); "Matched by name only: this may be another company." when a cited record matched by name only; and "A cited record was withdrawn by its source.", "A cited record was rejected by its source." or "A cited record was removed from its list." when a cited record is no longer stood by (not said again when the rule is `inactive`). In an open row each source shows a small outlined tag of its category after its host and its date ("Official source", "Trusted publisher", "Vendor's own page", "Unverified source"), and a record that its database no longer stands by shows what became of it ("Record withdrawn", "Record rejected", "Removed from the list"). A source with `quote_withheld` (a Brave result whose page could not be read, see [Brave text is never stored](#brave-text-is-never-stored)) shows its host link, no quote and the caption "Based on a search excerpt; the source page could not be read." (`SourceItem` in `FindingRow.tsx`); a blank quote never renders an empty quote block. A finding with no label, or with one the client does not know, shows no credibility line; a category or status the client does not know shows nothing.
- With a vendor chosen, the summary box also holds the latest run (`RunSummary`). The section reads the run once (`useLatestVendorResearch`) and hands it to `RunSummary` and to `ResearchCredits`, so that one timer follows it. The box says "Last researched: 10 Oct 2026", and has the link button **How it was researched** (**Hide research details** once open; `aria-expanded`) and **Research again** (`vendor.edit` only), which keeps its place whatever the run does. What a reader must not miss is on screen without a click: the error of a failed run, "Public databases turned off", one sentence for what the run could not check ("One source could not be checked, so results may be incomplete: SEC EDGAR.", or "2 sources could not be checked, so results may be incomplete: …"; `uncheckedSentence`), and a line for each list whose copy was out of date. Behind the button are the lines that say how the research works: under "What the last research found", one line per category ("Security incidents: nothing found") with its note; a category under which a list attests something in the vendor's favour (`attestationLines` in `vendorResearchText.ts`) reads "no risks found" instead of "nothing found" and shows one line per listing under it, never a finding: "Listed in the FedRAMP Marketplace: FedRAMP Authorized since 9 Sept 2024, impact level High. Matched by name only: this may be another company. Data as of 23 Nov 2026." (FedRAMP's status names and impact levels are kept as FedRAMP writes them; a status the screen does not know says only "Listed in the FedRAMP Marketplace."), or "Listed in the C2PA Conforming Products List: 2 conforming products, the newest since 9 Nov 2026."; a category with the outcome `over_cap` reads "Security incidents: risks found, left for the next run", with the note "More risks were found than one run keeps." under it (`describeCategoryLine` in `vendorResearchText.ts` builds the line from the category and the outcome only, never from the note; a run stored before `over_cap` existed, with `nothing_found` and that note, reads "nothing found" with the same sentence under it); under "What it checked", the coverage lines and the counters described below, and "Searched with Claude web search" for a run on Claude's search. A run that has none of these has no button. While the run is queued or running it shows "Researching…" and the latest run is polled every 3 seconds. Under it, a run that has written its progress shows what it is doing ("Searching the web: Security incidents", "Reading a page: Certifications", "Analysing what was found", or "Starting the research" before the first turn), the categories researched so far ("Researched so far: ...") and the counts ("Searches: 3, pages read: 2"), all built from closed words and numbers by `describeProgress` (`application/utils/vendorResearchText.ts`), never from text of the web. The polling window is counted from the latest sign of life, and its length follows the progress: 16 minutes while the run is queued (the 15 minutes the server lets a run wait for a place on the worker, `STALE_RUN_MINUTES`, and one more, so that a poll reads a run the sweep failed as `timed out`); 6 minutes while the research loop runs (it writes its progress after every search or read, and the longest still stretch is a turn of the model, cut off at 60 seconds, and the search or read after it, which only the loop's 5-minute clock cuts off: `LOOP_LIMITS`); 6 minutes while the run has not written its progress, is before its first turn (website check, hints, gathering) or is analysing, stretches in which the server writes none. The client keeps these server bounds as copies (`SERVER_STALE_RUN_MINUTES` and the loop deadlines in `useVendorResearch.ts`), and `useVendorResearch.serverBounds.test.ts` reads them from the server's source. A run that leaves the queue starts a fresh window, and a run that keeps moving (its `progress.updatedAt` changes) is followed to its end however long it takes, and a read that finds that a run has moved after polling stopped starts polling again. A run that makes no progress for its window is given up on: the screen says "Still running. Check back later." followed by **Check again** (a text button, shown to every role), which reads the run at once and polls for another window; and a run that is still queued or running is also read again when the browser tab regains focus (the app turns window-focus refetching off except for this, the count of the Research findings tab and the findings list under it). **Research again** is enabled again, so that the server decides. When a run that was seen active ends, the findings lists are refetched once, also when it ended while another vendor was chosen.
- **Credits.** `ResearchCredits` stands under the list, on screen without a click and also when nothing is pending, because a credit that sits behind a button is not the credit the terms of a source ask for: "News searched with the GDELT Project (last 3 months)", with "GDELT Project" as a link to the project, for a run that searched with it, and "Data sources: …", the attribution of each list the run asked or a finding on screen cites in its owner's words (of the 22 lists of `LISTS_SHOWN_WHEN_FOUND`, only of a list that offered a record, as the bullet on the coverage says further down), which is never translated. Nothing of a run is credited while it is going, or with all vendors listed, when no run is shown; the lists the findings on screen cite (`sources[].record.source_id`, `credibility_basis.sources[].record.source`) are credited in every case, so a finding from an earlier run keeps its credit when the latest run failed, is going or did not ask its list. The bullets further down on the counters and the search of a run, and on its coverage, list the lines of a run; where each of them is shown is as said here and in the bullet above.
- **Following a run.** A run started with the **Research risks** button on the vendors table is followed on the Research findings tab. The button leaves a one-shot hint in the query cache under its own key (`["vendorResearchFollow"]`, so invalidating the research queries does not clear it), for a started run and for an "already running" answer, not for a refusal. The findings section reads the hint when it mounts and opens on that vendor, even with nothing pending, as if `findingsVendorId` were in the URL, so `RunSummary` polls and the findings appear when the run ends. The URL parameter wins when both are present, and the hint is cleared by the first mount either way. It is not time-bound: it stays until the section mounts or the query cache collects it (10 minutes after the latest start). The start is answered some time after the click, and the person may have gone to the Research findings tab by then: the section is mounted and read its hint (none) when it mounted, so `useFollowedResearchVendorWhileOpen` listens to the query cache for a hint written while the section is open, uses it up and moves the section to that vendor, as the hint that was there at mount would have. A hint that was there at mount is not heard by it.
- The tab does not wait for the register of vendor risks: it has its own data, states and skeleton, so it stays as it is while the register reloads after the **Risk status** or the project filter changes. Only the register (Risks tab) and the heat map (Insights tab, which reads every active vendor risk, so the filters of the Risks tab do not reload it) give way to a skeleton. A decision, an accepted finding, and the end of a run that this tab is watching refresh the count of the tab with the lists; a run that ends while another tab is open is seen by the count at the next change of tab or window focus (see `usePendingFindingsCount`). The list (`useVendorRiskFindingPages`) is read again on window focus too (`refetchOnWindowFocus`), as the count is, so that the count of the tab and the list under it cannot disagree after the viewer comes back to the window.
- A run that is not active also shows one caption with its counters, "Searches: N, pages read: M" (from `searches_used` and `pages_read`), when they are not both zero, and one line that says which search it was (from `search_engine`): "Searched with Claude web search", "Searched with Brave Search" (neither needs a credit, so both stand with what the run did), or "News searched with the GDELT Project (last 3 months)" with "GDELT Project" as a link to the project, as the GDELT terms ask. A run with no engine (an older run, one that searched nothing, one none of whose searches was answered) shows no such line.
- A run shows its coverage after the category lines (`coverageLines` in `vendorResearchText.ts`), written for a buyer and naming no list twice: "Checked 33 public lists and registers. 1 had a record: FedRAMP Marketplace." (also "Checked 1 public list or register.", "None had a record." and "{count} had records: …"; a read-through source, such as the NVD, is a list here, dated by its answer), with only the lists that held a record named; "Also checked: Have I Been Pwned, the vendor's status page." for the web databases that answered (a run stored before the NVD was read through shows it there; a run that asked no list says "Checked: …" with them alone); "Not checked: …" with each list that could not be checked (it had no copy yet, or its read failed) and each web database that failed, so that a gap in the screening is never read as a clean result; "Data as of 22–23 Nov 2026.", the days of the copies the run asked once, as one range in the language of the screen; and the link **Show all lists** (**Hide the lists** once open, `aria-expanded`), which shows "No records: …" with every list the run asked that held nothing and nothing else; "{list}: last updated {date}, not up to date" for each stale list it asked; "Sources cited: N official, N trusted press, N vendor, N unverified"; "Public databases turned off" when every list was off; and "Data sources: …" (under the findings, on screen without a click: see **Credits** above), the attribution of each list it asked, and of each list that offered a record even if its read then failed (a read-through list, the NVD and the SEC among them, offers the records it had stored, and the findings may cite them), in its owner's words (of the 22 added on 2026-11-10, only of a list that offered a record), which is never translated. The names and attributions are the client's own, by id; those stored in the coverage are not read. A run without coverage (a run from before the credibility rules) shows none of it.
- **Research settings.** An Admin in an organization that has an LLM key sees a **Research settings** button beside the vendor filter. It opens `ResearchSettingsModal` (a `StandardModal`), mounted only while it is open, with three selects: **Research key** (default "Newest key (default)", with the helper text that Anthropic keys search with Claude's own web search, billed by Anthropic at about $10 per 1,000 searches (about 20 to 30 a run), and other keys search the news with Brave when the organization has a Brave search key, and otherwise the news of the last 3 months through the GDELT Project at no charge), **Analysis key** (default "Same as the research key (default)") and **Scheduled scan** (**Off**, **Every week**, **Every 2 weeks** or **Every month**, with a sentence that says the scan researches every vendor again on that schedule, tells the assignee when it finds something, analyses only the sources it has not seen and uses the keys above). The other options are the organization's keys, newest first, written `<provider> · <model>` (data, not translated). Nothing is asked of the server until the window opens, and the settings are never requested for anyone else (they would be a 403 and a toast). The selects appear once the saved choice and the key list are there; what the person changes is kept apart from what is saved, and a saved choice that names a key which is not in the list shows as the default, and an interval that is not one of the choices shows as off. **Save** sends all three fields (`null` for a default key and for a scan that is off) and, when it works, puts the answer in the cache, announces "Research settings saved." and closes. A failure leaves the window open with the choice; the global error toast reports it, except a 404 (a chosen key was deleted meanwhile), which it skips on purpose, so the window says "One of the chosen LLM keys no longer exists. Choose the keys again." and reads the key list again. A failed load shows **Retry** in the window. Under the research key a line says where its news comes from (`GET /v1/vendor-research/search-status`, read each time the window opens, without a toast on failure). A key searched with Brave (engine `brave`) gets the quiet line "News is searched with Brave.". A key that gets its news from GDELT alone gets the line "This key gets news only from GDELT, which sometimes refuses requests."; when the organization's latest runs that say something about the news all got none it becomes a warning that starts with "Recent research runs got no news." Either points to a Brave search key: the link "Add a Brave search key for more reliable news" to the search key form (`SEARCH_KEY_CREATE_PATH`, `/settings/apikeys?addSearchKey=1`), or, while a change in the window is unsaved, "Save, then add a Brave search key for more reliable news." without a link (leaving would drop the change), followed by "Or choose an Anthropic key." when the organization has one. When the status already counts a Brave key (`braveConfigured`) and a GDELT key is still on screen, only "Choose an Anthropic key for more reliable news." is offered, when the organization has one. An Anthropic key, and a status that could not be read, show no line.
- **Search API key (Settings → API keys).** `SettingsPage/SearchKey`, under the LLM keys, shown with `llmKeys.admin` and only where the LLM keys are shown (not in the super admin console, which has no organization). Its title is "Search API key" and its one sentence says that vendor research searches the news with Brave when the research key is not an Anthropic key, and that without a Brave key news comes from the GDELT Project, which sometimes refuses requests. It shows "Brave Search · ending in 1234" or "Not set", with **Add key**, or **Replace key** and **Remove**; **Remove** asks first in a `ConfirmationModal` ("Remove the Brave search key?", "Vendor research will search the news with the GDELT Project instead."). The add and replace form (a `StandardModal`) takes the key in a password `Field`, links to `https://brave.com/search/api/` in a new tab ("Get a key at brave.com/search/api") and states Brave's price. A 422 `invalid_key` says "Brave rejected this key. Check that you copied all of it."; a 422 `unreachable` says "Could not reach Brave to check this key." and offers **Save anyway**, which sends the key again with `skipCheck: true`; any other failure says "Could not save the key. Try again.". `?addSearchKey=1` (`SEARCH_KEY_CREATE_PATH`) opens the form once and is then dropped from the URL, as `?addKey=1` does for the LLM key form.
